## 0.1 The absolute non-resolution rule

**[P] Rule H-1 (Non-Resolution).** This instrument does **NOT** select, determine, confirm, imply or prepare the ground for selecting:

- Krytos's legal form
- Sewak Sathi Sanstha's legal form
- any parent / subsidiary structure
- any ownership structure
- founder ownership
- board, council or governing-body authority
- any control arrangement
- IP ownership
- any succession mechanism

**unless the source corpus already contains that decision, in which case it is quoted with its ID and its own status label — and a quotation is not a selection.**

**Where the corpus is silent:**

> **`UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`**

**Where the authority to decide is itself unknown:**

> **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`**

**[P] Rule H-2 (Five Assumptions Expressly Refused).** The brief instructs that five propositions must not be assumed. **Each is refused here, and each is classified in Part I against the evidence:**

| Refused assumption | Where it is tested |
|---|---|
| That **"Krytos" means a company** | §I.4 negative findings; **KP-04**; Part II Model A/D |
| That **"Krytos" means a parent company** | **KP-04, KP-05, KP-06**; Part II Model C; Part XXI **NS-01** |
| That **Krytos is already legally constituted** | **KP-40…KP-44** (§I.6); Part II Model D |
| That **the founder owns Krytos** | **KP-09, KP-11, KP-12**; Part XXI **NS-02, NS-03** |
| That **Sewak Sathi is legally subordinate to Krytos** | **KP-04, KP-07**; Part II Model C; Part XIV |

**[P] Rule H-3 (Repetition Is Not Establishment).** The governing analytical rule of this instrument, and the reason Part XXI exists:

> **A proposition that appears in seven documents may still have been decided in none of them.** Where every occurrence of a statement traces back to a single upstream act — or to a single unsupplied document — the statement is **one** fact repeated, not seven facts. **Architectural frequency is not legal establishment.**

**[P] Rule H-4 (No Legalisation By Wording).** Inherited from **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 drafting rule]** and **[S-E: Rule E-59]**. No phrase in this document converts *"parent/company context"*, *"ownership ecosystem"*, *"Krytos-owned"* or *"under Krytos"* into a legal proposition. **Each is recorded as the words the source used, and classified.**

**[P] Rule H-5 (No Invention).** Krytos's **legal status, jurisdiction, ownership, governance, succession, IP position, data position, banking position and money-flow legality** are not invented, estimated, inferred to a conclusion, or filled by analogy. **Rule G-6 of v1.3-G is inherited: no number, threshold, budget, percentage, salary, date of incorporation, shareholding, fee or value is created.**

**[P] Rule H-6 (Source ID Supremacy).** Inherited from **[S-F: Rule F-4]**. No source ID is replaced, renumbered or retired. **v1.3-G's IDs (`UE-nn`, `GC-nn`, `GPD-n`, `OPT-n`, `FC-nn`, `G-nn`) are cited as they stand.** This instrument mints only its own `H-` series (§0.4).

## 0.2 The classification scheme

**[P]** Every proposition about Krytos in this instrument carries exactly one of five classifications. **The scheme is the brief's, adopted verbatim.**

| Classification | Definition | Test applied |
|---|---|---|
| **SOURCE-ESTABLISHED** | The corpus records it as an **existing decision or an existing fact**, tagged `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` or stated as a preservation of a source decision | Is there a status label, or a §1.1 preservation row, that says this **is** so — rather than that it **should be** so? |
| **SOURCE-PROPOSED** | The corpus records it as a **recommendation, model, proposal or draft** requiring approval | Does the text carry `PROPOSED`, `REQUIRES FINAL APPROVAL`, `NOT FINAL`, `[P]`, or *"recommended"*? |
| **SOURCE-ASSUMED** | The corpus **relies on it without deciding it** — it appears as a premise, a parenthetical, a phrase in a chain, or an unexamined consequence | Would removing the proposition break a sentence that never argues for it? **This is the most important class, and the one Part XXI audits** |
| **UNKNOWN** | The corpus raises the question and records no answer | Is there an `UNRESOLVED`, `[V]`, `UNKNOWN` or *"not stated"* against it? |
| **OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS** | The corpus **names a document, fact or instrument that is not supplied** | Does the answer exist only in a document the corpus refers to but does not contain? |

**[P] Rule H-7 (A Preserved Decision Is Established As A Decision, Not As A Fact).** Where v1.1 §1.1 records *"Krytos owns the organisation"* with the status *"Preserved as APPROVED ARCHITECTURE"*, the classification is: **SOURCE-ESTABLISHED as an architectural decision** — and **OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS as to its legal basis**, because the instrument that made it (v1.0) is not supplied. **Both classifications are recorded. Neither is collapsed into the other.**

## 0.3 Status labels and evidence tags

**[S]** The five v1.2 status labels (`APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` · `PROPOSED / REQUIRES FINAL APPROVAL` · `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` · `UNRESOLVED` · `FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION`) and the evidence tags (`[S] [A] [P] [C] [I] [V] [X] [M] [H] [SC]`) are used exactly as defined at **[S: App. A §Document Control]** and throughout v1.3-A…v1.3-G. **Rule G-10 of v1.3-G is inherited: no `[X]` is promoted to a conclusion.**

# MANDATORY EXECUTIVE ANSWER

> ## The primary question, answered from the corpus and from nothing else.

**Question:** *What does "under Krytos" actually mean in the existing architecture, and what minimum facts must be established before Sewak Sathi Sanstha can validly be evaluated as an entity operating under, owned by, controlled by, affiliated with, or independent from Krytos?*

| # | Answer |
|---|---|
| **1** | **"Under Krytos" has no single meaning in the corpus. It performs at least four distinct functions, and the architecture never separates them.** It is: **(a)** a **hierarchical position** — the top box of the master chain `KRYTOS → SEWAK SATHI SANSTHA → NATIONAL → STATE → CITY → MOHALLA → VERIFIED MEMBERS` **[S: Ch.1, Ch.4]**; **(b)** an **ownership statement** — *"The organisation is owned by Krytos under founder/CEO Vasu, **subject to the final legal structure**"* **[S: Const. Art.3]**; **(c)** a **descriptive label for a context, not a person** — *"Parent/company context and **ownership ecosystem**"* **[S: Ch.1]**, *"KRYTOS (**Owner / product ecosystem**)"* **[S: App. A §3.3 Diagram 3B]**; and **(d)** a **brand tier** — *"**T2 PARENT** — Krytos — Ownership and the wider organisation context"* **[S-E: §12.2]**, whose status is recorded as *"**APPROVED as parent context**"* **[S-E: Annexure F]**. **Functions (a), (c) and (d) are not legal propositions. Only (b) is — and (b) is expressly conditional.** |
| **2** | **The single most important provenance finding: every "Krytos owns" statement in the corpus traces to one upstream document that is not supplied.** **[S: App. A §1.1]** states, verbatim: *"The source studied for this master specification is the attached file named **Krytos_Organisation_Master_Architecture.pdf, a 21-page working master architecture**"* — and its decision table records *"**Krytos owns the organisation; founder/CEO Vasu is identified as founder and may work operationally** — Preserved as **APPROVED ARCHITECTURE**."* The v1.1 front matter gives the source base as *"**KRYTOS ORGANISATION MASTER ARCHITECTURE, CONSTITUTION & LAUNCH SPECIFICATION v1.0**"*, and the v1.1 public-name note says *"KRYTOS remains the parent/company context and ownership context **already established in v1.0**."* **v1.0 is not in the corpus.** **Rule H-3 and Rule H-7 therefore apply: the ownership proposition is SOURCE-ESTABLISHED *as an architectural decision preserved from v1.0*, and OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS *as to its legal basis*.** |
| **3** | **Three further documents are named by the corpus and are not supplied.** **(i)** `Krytos_Organisation_Master_Architecture.pdf`, 21 pp. — the v1.1 source; **(ii)** `KRYTOS ORGANISATION MASTER ARCHITECTURE, CONSTITUTION & LAUNCH SPECIFICATION v1.0` — the v1.1 source base; **(iii)** `Zero-Capital Local Venture Ecosystem — Master Architecture and Implementation Blueprint, Working Draft v1.0`, of which **[S: App. A §1.1]** says *"**The full separate blueprint file was not attached in this task**"* — **this is the origin of `LDR-U17`**. **(i) and (ii) are probably one document under two citation forms (H-PD-1 records the ambiguity rather than resolving it).** **No Krytos constitutive document of any kind is in the supplied corpus.** |
| **4** | **"Krytos" denotes three different kinds of object, and the corpus never distinguishes them.** **(α) An organisation** — the v1.0 title is *"KRYTOS **ORGANISATION** MASTER ARCHITECTURE, CONSTITUTION & LAUNCH SPECIFICATION"*, and the v1.1 title becomes *"KRYTOS **/** SEWAK SATHI SANSTHA …"*; **(β) A parent** — *"parent/company context"*, *"preserving Krytos parent context"*, *"T2 PARENT"*; **(γ) An ecosystem or portfolio** — *"the broader Krytos ecosystem together with **other Krytos projects such as LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge and Trade Lab**"* **[S: App. A §3.1]**, *"ownership ecosystem"* **[S: Ch.1]**, *"Owner / **product ecosystem**"* **[Diagram 3B]**. **A reading of (α) makes Krytos the organisation itself, in which case "under Krytos" is not a relationship at all. A reading of (β) makes it a parent entity. A reading of (γ) makes it a label for a portfolio of projects with no necessary legal personality.** **Part II constructs all four models; Part XXI audits which is being relied on where.** |
| **5** | **Four sibling projects are named exactly once in 3.87 MB of corpus and never again.** `LifeXP`, `Code Forge`, `App Forge` and `Trade Lab` appear only at **[S: App. A §3.1]** and in Diagram 3A. **No instrument — not the stress test, not v1.3-A…F, not v1.3-G — mentions any of them again.** Their legal form, their relationship to Sewak Sathi, their shared resources, their staff, their contracts, their data and their brands are **`OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS`**. **This matters because any parent/holding or common-control model (R2, R3, R7) is a model about a *group*, and the corpus contains a group of five things about which it describes one.** |
| **6** | **The corpus itself states that the parent's owner is outside the reach of Sewak Sathi's constitution.** **[S-A: A-13 §12.2]**, on who can be bound by a legally enforceable Tier-1 restriction: *"The entity, its directors/trustees/owners, and possibly successor entities — **but *not* an unrelated owner of the parent unless instrumented**."* And **[S-A: A-13 §12.4]**, on what a mission lock CANNOT do: *"**Prevent a determined owner with legal control from restructuring around it [V]**"*; *"Create rights in third parties unless an instrument does so [V]"*; *"**Bind a successor who controls the entity and rejects it, absent an enforceable instrument**."* **This is the corpus's own answer to the question "does the Krytos relationship protect the mission?" — and the answer is: not without an instrument, and no instrument exists.** |
| **7** | **What the corpus establishes, proposes, assumes and leaves unknown — Part II in one line each.** **MODEL A (established):** Krytos is the top of the operating chain; ownership of the organisation sits with *"Krytos under founder/CEO Vasu"* `APPROVED` **subject to the final legal structure**; the Council is not owner above the CEO unless a legal charter says so; the founder/CEO has *"broad executive flexibility **within final legal structure**"*; States may not alter legal structures. **MODEL B (proposed):** a CEO–Council–National Team model in which the Founder/CEO's proposed role expressly includes **"brand ownership"** and the National Team **cannot "finalise legal structure"**; four structure options; a brand tier in which the parent is named accurately in legal contexts and subordinated in service contexts. **MODEL C (assumed):** that Krytos is a legal person; that it is a *company*; that it can own another entity; that its governing documents exist and will operate on the founder's death; that it can fund the centre; that it holds or can hold the IP; that the data fiduciary is *"presumably … ultimately Krytos"*; that one person owns it. **MODEL D (unknown):** everything in Part III — 28 questions, of which **1 is answered, 1 is proposed, 25 are unknown and 1 is authority-unresolved** (with a second item, H-Q20, carrying an authority-unresolved dependency). |
| **8** | **`UE-24` decomposes into 28 questions and only one of them is answered by the corpus.** Part III. **Answered: 1** — H-Q27, *"who is the founder and CEO?"* → **Vasu** **[S-D: §27.1]**, and even that answer is a given name with no surname, no address and no office anywhere in 3.87 MB. **Proposed: 1** (H-Q18 — Krytos as a *candidate* funder; the authority to advance is itself asked by **[S-B: EV-4]**). **Unknown: 25.** **Authority unresolved: 1** (H-Q28 — who may decide the Krytos relationship), **plus H-Q20**, which is unknown *and* authority-unresolved by dependency on **[S-F: C-D-17]**. **Not one of the 28 has an answer that establishes Krytos's legal identity.** |
| **9** | **The corpus's own word for the relationship is *"presumably"*.** **[S-A: §11.2]**, Data-estate row: *"the legal data fiduciary is **presumably** the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**."* **[A]** That single adverb is the most honest statement of the Krytos relationship in 3.87 MB — and it is an inference by v1.3-A, marked as such, not a determination. **Part XXI NS-06 records it.** |
| **10** | **GC-01 is not resolved by this paper, and Part VII shows why it cannot be resolved without KDP-04.** The waterfall's priority 4 (*founder compensation*) and priority 8 (*residual surplus / founder upside*) are `APPROVED` and reserved; **[S: App. A §3.2]** records that a nonprofit form carries *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside."* **But who the "founder" is *paid by*, and who is entitled to "founder upside", depends on whether Krytos is a person, a label, or the same object as the organisation — i.e. on KDP-01 and KDP-04.** The GC-01 decision tree at Part VII has **nine nodes, of which eight end in `UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED` or `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`.** |
| **11** | **The minimum facts required are twelve, and ten of them are held by one person.** **KDP-01 … KDP-12** (Part XV). **Ten require disclosure of documents or facts about Krytos** — legal status, jurisdiction, governing documents, ownership/control, succession, IP, brand, data authority, banking authority, relationship with Sewak Sathi. **Two require decisions** — KDP-11 (Krytos's authority over Sewak Sathi) and KDP-12 (Sewak Sathi's independence conditions). **Part XVII finds that for KDP-01…KDP-10 the DECIDER is the founder/CEO as discloser, but for KDP-11 and KDP-12 the decider is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`, because the body that would decide Sewak Sathi's subordination is the body whose own standing is `LDR-U01`.** |
| **12** | **Does Krytos introduce an additional governance layer? Yes — and it is the only layer in the architecture that has no rules at all.** The corpus specifies authority for seven levels (Krytos/Founder-CEO, Council, National, State, City, Mohalla, Members) and gives **Krytos no responsibilities, no outputs, no decision rows, no escalation route, no RACI line and no meeting cadence.** **[S: App. A §4.2]** lists National responsibilities across ten domains; there is no equivalent for Krytos. **[S-D: Part 34]**'s authority map has **no Krytos row**. **[S: App. E]**'s approval matrix has **no Krytos row**. **Krytos is a box in a diagram with an ownership statement attached and no governance content whatsoever.** |
| **13** | **Pilot consequence.** Of fourteen domains tested at Part XX: **6 BLOCK THE PILOT** (entity formation, banking, worker compensation, IP, data, contracts) · **5 CONDITIONAL** (insurance, governance, public representation, business revenue, pilot authorisation) · **1 DOES NOT BLOCK** (safeguarding capability — which is entity-independent but whose *holder* is not) · **2 UNKNOWN** (social funds, reserve custody — because no fund exists yet to be affected). **The Krytos relationship does not add a new blocker; it makes `SR-01` unanswerable until KDP-01…KDP-10 are supplied.** |
| **14** | **Final verdict** — # **D — KRYPTOS RELATIONSHIP CANNOT YET BE DEFINED.** Part XXIII |
| **15** | **Why D and not C** — **C would mean the question is not yet decidable for want of a method.** It is not: the question is now precisely defined (Part III), the models are enumerated (Parts II and IV), the consequences are mapped (Parts V–XIV), the facts required are listed (Part XV), the professionals are briefed (Part XVI) and the authority position is stated (Part XVII). **What is missing is not method — it is ten facts that exist in the world and are held by one person, plus one document that exists and was not supplied.** **A question whose only missing inputs are undisclosed facts is not "not yet decision-ready"; it is undefined until those facts are disclosed.** |
| **16** | **Why D and not B** — **B requires that the relationship be describable with material gaps.** It is not describable at all: the corpus does not establish whether Krytos **is** a legal person, so it cannot be said whether Sewak Sathi would be *under* it, *beside* it, *inside* it or *the same thing as* it. **R1 through R8 remain mutually exclusive and none can be excluded on the present record.** |
| **17** | **What would move D → B, and B → A** — **D → B:** supply **KDP-01, KDP-02, KDP-03, KDP-04** — Krytos's legal status, jurisdiction, governing documents and ownership/control. Four disclosures, all held by one person, none requiring counsel. **B → A:** add **KDP-05…KDP-10** plus the **v1.0 document**, and obtain **H-PV-1/H-PV-2/H-PV-3** opinions on the resulting relationship. **No architectural work is required at any point.** |
| **18** | **The final principle, applied** — **"Under Krytos" is currently functioning as an architectural shortcut for an undefined legal and governance relationship.** It carries ownership, hierarchy, brand tier, funding expectation, IP default and data-fiduciary presumption — **six loads — on a phrase that the corpus itself qualifies with *"subject to the final legal structure"* and *"presumably."*** **Either it receives a precise, validated meaning, or the architecture must stop relying on it. This paper makes the first possible; it does not perform it, and it does not perform the second.** |

**Relationship terms also harvested:** `parent` · `company` · `organisation` · `founder` · `CEO` · `owner` · `ownership` · `subsidiary` · `affiliate` · `controlled entity` · `group` · `holding` · `IP` · `brand` · `platform` · `software` · `assets` · `data` · `succession` · `governance` · `council` · `board` · `legal structure` · `registration` · `incorporation` · `jurisdiction` · `constitution` · `charter` · `articles` · `operating entity`.

## I.2 The Krytos provenance table — KP-01 … KP-44

**Columns exactly as the brief requires: ID · Source · Section · Exact Source Meaning · Status · Consequence.**

**[P] Rule H-8 (Ambiguity Is Not Paraphrased Away).** Where the source wording is ambiguous, **the ambiguity is the entry.** The compound phrase *"parent/company context"* is reproduced, not normalised to "parent company." The conditional *"subject to the final legal structure"* is reproduced with every ownership statement, not dropped for brevity.

### I.2.1 v1.1 — front matter, source integrity and identity **[S: App. A]**

| ID | Source | Section | Exact source meaning | Status | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## I.4 Term-harvest results — what the relationship vocabulary actually returns

**[A]** The brief requires that 30 relationship terms be harvested. **The result is as important for what returns nothing as for what returns something.**

| Term | Result across the corpus | Classification consequence |
|---|---|---|

## I.5 Five findings from the harvest

**[A]** Each is derived only from §I.2–§I.4.

| # | Finding | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **The corpus describes a group of five projects and specifies one of them.** `LifeXP`, `Code Forge`, `App Forge` and `Trade Lab` appear in **exactly one passage** — **[S: App. A §3.1]** and Diagram 3A — and are **never mentioned again by any of the eight instruments**, including the 3.87 MB of amendments and the stress test that examined federation, scaling, liability, brand, data and succession. **No instrument states whether they share staff, systems, premises, money, data, brand or liability with Sewak Sathi.** | §I.4 last row |
| **2** | **The ownership proposition has exactly one origin, and it is not in the corpus.** Every `APPROVED` ownership statement — **[S: Const. Art.3]**, **[S: App. A §2.1]**, **[S: Ch.32]**, **[S: Ch.4]** — is a **preservation** of **[S: App. A §1.1 row 1]**, which is a **preservation** of the 21-page `Krytos_Organisation_Master_Architecture.pdf`, which is **not supplied**. **Rule H-3: seven documents repeating one preserved decision is one decision, not seven.** | KP-02, KP-03, KP-11, KP-28, KP-37 |
| **3** | **The corpus uses at least six non-equivalent formulations of the ownership relationship, and never reconciles them.** (i) *"The organisation is **owned by Krytos** under founder/CEO Vasu"* **[Art.3]**; (ii) *"The founder / CEO, Vasu, **owns the organisation through Krytos**"* **[Exec. Summary]**; (iii) *"**CEO owns through Krytos**"* **[§1.2]**; (iv) *"ownership remains with **CEO/Krytos**"* **[§1.1]**; (v) *"Ownership — Primary holder: **Krytos / CEO**"* **[S: Ch.4]**; (vi) *"**Krytos / Founder-CEO**"* as a single box **[S: Ch.4]**. **Formulations (i) and (ii) point in opposite directions**: in (i) Krytos owns and the founder is below; in (ii) the founder owns and Krytos is the vehicle. **Both are `APPROVED`-level source text.** | KP-03, KP-05, KP-07, KP-10, KP-11, KP-21, KP-28, KP-35, KP-36 |
| **4** | **Krytos has no governance content whatsoever.** It has: one box in two diagrams, one ownership article, one authority-table row, one brand tier, one decision-register row, and one validation question. It has **no** responsibilities (§4.2 has ten National domains and no Krytos section), **no** outputs, **no** decision row in the approval matrix (**[S: App. E]** — 20 rows, no Krytos), **no** row in the authority map (**[S-D: Part 34]** — 30 rows, no Krytos), **no** escalation route, **no** meeting cadence, **no** officers, **no** RACI line (**[S-D: §34.2]** — 15 activities, no Krytos column), and **no** succession provision. | KP-25, KP-31, KP-90; §I.4 |
| **5** | **The corpus's most definite statement about the parent's reach is that Sewak Sathi's constitution cannot bind the parent's owner.** *"**not an unrelated owner of the parent unless instrumented**"* **[S-A: A-13 §12.2]**; *"**Prevent a determined owner with legal control from restructuring around it [V]**"* **[S-A: A-13 §12.4]**. **Combined with finding 4 — Krytos has no governance content — the architecture has a layer above it that it cannot bind and does not describe.** | KP-71, KP-72 |

## I.6 Provenance defects and citation clarifications — H-PD-1 … H-PD-10

**[P]** **[S-F: PD-1…PD-9]** and **[S-G: GPD-1…GPD-4]** are carried forward unchanged and are not restated. **Ten further items are recorded.**

| ID | Type | Finding | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **H-PD-1** | **Citation ambiguity — unresolved, not resolved** | The upstream document is named **three ways**: `Krytos_Organisation_Master_Architecture.pdf, a 21-page working master architecture` **[S: App. A §1.1]**; `KRYTOS ORGANISATION MASTER ARCHITECTURE, CONSTITUTION & LAUNCH SPECIFICATION v1.0` **[v1.1 front matter "Source base"]**; and `v1.0 Krytos Master Architecture` **[Architecture Brief]**. **Probably one document; possibly two** (a 21-page working paper and a fuller v1.0 specification). **This instrument does not resolve which.** | **KDP-03 must obtain whichever exists, and must record whether they are the same document.** Until then, the origin of the ownership decision is ambiguous |
| **H-PD-2** | **Compound phrase, never defined** | *"**parent/company context**"* appears twice (**[S: Ch.1]**, **[S: App. A public-name note]**) and is the corpus's primary description of Krytos. **The slash is not defined.** It may mean *"parent, i.e. company"*, *"parent-or-company"*, or *"parent/company [as opposed to something else]"*. A fourth phrase, *"**ownership ecosystem**"*, and a fifth, *"**Owner / product ecosystem**"*, are not equivalent to either | **Rule H-8: the ambiguity is preserved. Part II Model A records the words; Model D records what they do not establish** |
| **H-PD-3** | **Hierarchy inconsistency** | **[S: App. A §1.1]**'s preserved hierarchy row is *"National Council / Governance Layer, National Team, State Teams, City Teams, Mohalla Teams, Verified Members"* — **Krytos and the founder do not appear.** **[S: App. A §3.3 Diagram 3B]** puts `KRYTOS` at the top, then `Founder / CEO`, then the Council. **[S: Ch.1]** and **[S: Ch.4]** put `KRYTOS / Founder-CEO` at the top as one box. **[S-B: §3.2]** gives *"Krytos / Founder-CEO → National Team → …"* — **omitting the Council**. **[S-ST: §5]** counts *"**Seven levels** (Krytos → Council → National → State → City → Mohalla → Members)"* | **Four different hierarchies. Whether Krytos is a *level* (with latency and management, per [S-ST: §5]) or an *owner* (outside the operating chain) is `UNKNOWN` — and it changes what "under" means** |
| **H-PD-4** | **Diagram versus text** | Diagram 3B places **KRYTOS above Founder/CEO**; the Executive Summary says the founder *"**owns the organisation through Krytos**"* and Art.3 says *"owned by Krytos **under** founder/CEO Vasu."* **The diagram and the text order the two differently** | **Rule H-8. Part II Model C records both readings** |
| **H-PD-5** | **Omission from the two complete authority instruments** | **[S: App. E]** Approval & Delegation Matrix (20 decision rows) and **[S-D: Part 34 §34.1]** authority map (30 rows) are the corpus's exhaustive authority instruments. **Neither contains a Krytos row.** Neither contains a row for "who may act for the owner" | **Any Krytos authority is undefined even at the level of *proposal*. Part XI** |
| **H-PD-6** | **File-name provenance** | Both supplied/quoted file names begin `KRYTOS_` — `KRYTOS_SEWAK_SATHI_SANSTHA_…v1_2.pdf` and `KRYTOS_SEWAK_SATHI_SANSTHA_MASTER_ARCHITECTURE_…v1_1.pdf`. **The documents are about Sewak Sathi and titled Krytos** | **A naming practice, not a legal fact. Recorded because it is the most repeated occurrence of the word in the corpus and asserts nothing (Rule H-3)** |
| **H-PD-7** | **The undefined slash** | `KRYTOS / Founder-CEO` **[S: Ch.4]** · `Krytos / Founder-CEO Vasu` **[v1.1 "Prepared for"]** · `Krytos / CEO` **[S: Ch.4 authority table]** · `CEO/Krytos` **[S: App. A §1.1]** · `Founder/Krytos-owned` **[S-E: N-E-17]**. **Five occurrences of a slash joining a context and a person, in both orders** | **The corpus cannot say whether Krytos and the founder are one actor, two actors, or an actor and a vehicle. This is the single most consequential ambiguity in Part I. Part XXI NS-01** |
| **H-PD-8** | **Chain truncation** | **[S-B: §3.2]**'s chain omits the National Council; **[S-E: §12.1]**'s omits Krytos's sub-structure; **[S-ST: §5]**'s counts seven levels including both | See H-PD-3 |
| **H-PD-9** | **Status of the Architecture Brief** | `Sewak_Sathi_Sanstha_Architecture_Brief.md` is a **reader's working summary** of v1.2 (*"my working understanding"*), not a source instrument. It is the only place that names the lineage *"v1.0 Krytos Master Architecture → v1.1 → v1.2"* | **Cited in this instrument as `[Brief]` and never as source authority. Its lineage statement is corroborated by KP-41 and KP-02, which are source** |
| **H-PD-10** | **"Constitution" is used of two objects** | The v1.0 title contains *"CONSTITUTION"*; **[S: App. A §36]** is *"Final Organisational Constitution"* (19 articles, Sewak Sathi's); **[S-ST: §5]** requires *"a **common constitution**"* for a federation; **[S-E: Rule E-58]** requires naming *"the legal entity"* in legal contexts | **Which constitution governs Krytos is `UNKNOWN`. KDP-03** |

# PART II — DEFINING THE CURRENT KRYPTOS MODEL

**[P]** Four models, strictly separated as the brief requires. **Rule H-9 (No Model Bleed):** nothing from Model B may appear in Model A; nothing from Model C may appear in Model A or B; Model D contains everything the other three cannot support.

**[P]** **Source-established facts only.** Each carries its ID and its own status label. **Nothing in this model is an inference.**

Rule H-5: no magnitude is adopted** |

**[A] Model A, summarised in one sentence:** **Krytos is a named context at the top of an operating chain, described as owner of the organisation under a founder/CEO, expressly subject to a legal structure that has not been chosen, containing four other projects that are never described, possessing governing documents that are never produced, and sitting above a constitution that — by the corpus's own statement — cannot bind its owner.**

**[A] What Model A does NOT contain:** no legal form · no jurisdiction · no registration · no officers · no members or shareholders · no capital · no bank account · no assets · no IP · no contracts · no employees · no tax position · no governance body · no meeting · no decision · no succession provision · no responsibility · no output · no row in either authority instrument.

## II.2 MODEL B — WHAT KRYPTOS IS PROPOSED TO BE

**[P]** Proposals only. **Every item is `PROPOSED` and, per [S-F: Rule F-9], has no operative force.**

| # | Proposal | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B-1** | **A CEO–Council–National Team model in which the Founder/CEO's role expressly includes *"brand ownership"*** — alongside *"final executive authority, founder vision, crisis escalation, major appointments, national expansion decisions."* | **[S: App. A §3.5]** | `PROPOSED / REQUIRES FINAL APPROVAL`. **This is the only assignment of brand ownership to anyone in the corpus — and `A-N12` records brand ownership as `UNRESOLVED`** |
| **B-2** | **The National Council may not approve alone: *"Ownership transfer, override of CEO outside charter, direct operational micromanagement, spending outside approved budget."*** | **[S: App. A §3.5]** | `PROPOSED`. **Implies that *somebody* may approve an ownership transfer; the row does not say who** |
| **B-3** | **The National Team may not *"finalise legal structure."*** | **[S: App. A §3.5]** | `PROPOSED` |
| **B-4** | **The Founder/CEO may not approve alone *"final legal/tax conclusions without professionals."*** | **[S: App. A §3.5]** | `PROPOSED` — **and v1.3-G Rule G-1 adopts it as an operating rule** |
| **B-5** | **Council should be *"an oversight/standards/advisory body with delegated powers, while CEO retains final executive authority **subject to law and charter**"* — approval required: *"Yes — governance charter."*** | **[S: App. A §1.2]** contradiction review | `PROPOSED` — **the charter does not exist** |
| **B-6** | **Krytos as brand tier T2 PARENT, subordinated in service contexts and named accurately in legal, financial, contractual, regulatory, funding and accountability contexts.** | **[S-E: E-11, Rule E-58]** | `PROPOSED — BLOCKED BY UNRESOLVED ITEM` (N12, N13) |
| **B-7** | **No concealment on request: *"Any person asking who owns or controls the organisation receives an accurate answer in plain language, in their language, immediately, from any role-holder … An ownership structure that can only survive not being asked about is not a governance structure."*** | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** | `PROPOSED`. **Today the accurate answer is A-4's conditional sentence** |
| **B-8** | **Four structure options, one of which — *"Internal Krytos division"* — would make Sewak Sathi not a separate person at all.** | **[S: App. A §3.2]** | `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`; *"Option for early internal pilot **only after validation**"* |
| **B-9** | **A hybrid or two-entity structure with *"a brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"*.** | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | `PROPOSED` `[V]` — a RECONSIDERATION item |
| **B-10** | **A holding structure *"with the mission held separately from both."*** | **[S-ST: R4 (iii)]** | `PROPOSED` `[V]` |
| **B-11** | **A mission-lock instrument, with four ascending options including *"an irrevocable purpose clause in the governing document of **the operating entity**"* and *"a federated structure where the mission is held by a body that no single shareholder controls."*** | **[S-ST: F-11 item 7]**; **[S-A: A-13]** | `PROPOSED` `[P, V]` — *"requires … **the founder's own willingness — which is a decision, not an analysis**"* |
| **B-12** | **Reserved matters — seven items including *"change of mission, name, brand or **entity structure**"* — not delegable to any interim authority.** | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-D: LDR-C26]** | `PROPOSED`; **holder `UNRESOLVED`** |
| **B-13** | **Federation at scale: *"legally distinct state entities bound by a common constitution, standards body and brand licence"* which *"dilutes single-owner control … **reduces founder flexibility — a deliberate trade**."*** | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** | `PROPOSED` `[V]`; classified **LATER** by **[S-A: A-L7]** |
| **B-14** | **Krytos as a candidate funder of the centre: *"founder capital / **Krytos** / validated donation / accrued-and-waived fee."*** | **[S-B: E-03, B-03, §9]** | `PROPOSED` — the decision itself is `UNRESOLVED — no source instrument` |
| **B-15** | **Brand ownership alternatives: *"Founder/Krytos-owned / entity-owned / licensed / held in trust."*** | **[S-E: N-E-17]** | `UNRESOLVED — CARRIED FORWARD` |

**[A] Model B, summarised:** **every structural proposition about Krytos in the corpus is a proposal. Not one has been adopted. [S-F: Rule F-9]: "a proposal that has not been approved has no operative force."**

## II.3 MODEL C — WHAT THE SEWAK SATHI DOCUMENTS *ASSUME* KRYPTOS TO BE

**[P]** **This is the most important model in Part II.** Each row identifies a proposition the architecture **relies on** without ever deciding, and states where the reliance occurs. **Rule H-3: an assumption may appear repeatedly without ever having been formally decided.**

| # | Assumption | Where it is relied on | Is it decided anywhere? | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C-1** | **That Krytos is a legal person** — something capable of owning, of being a parent, of having governing documents, of having its own succession | *"owned by Krytos"* **[Art.3]**; *"Krytos's governing documents"* **[S-A: A-14 S5]**; *"Krytos's own succession"* **[S-A: §11.2]**; *"subsidiary"* **[§3.2]**; *"Internal Krytos **division**"* | **NO.** H-Q02 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-2** | **That Krytos is a *company*** | *"parent/**company** context"* **[S: Ch.1]**; Rule E-58's rationale *"a **corporate** owner"* **[S-E]**; *"a **corporate** frame"* **[S-E: §12]**; *"no **golden-share/protective-vote** mechanism"* **[S-ST: F-11]**; *"no single **shareholder** controls"* **[S-ST: F-11 (iv)]**; *"the governing document of the **operating entity**"* **[same]** | **NO.** `company` is never predicated of Krytos in a declarative sentence (§I.4) | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-3** | **That the founder owns Krytos** | *"**Whoever owns Krytos** owns the operating system of the institution"* **[S-A: §11.2]**; *"**one person's ownership of the parent**"* **[S-ST: F-11]**; *"a **future owner of Krytos**"* **[S-ST: §1]**; *"under **one private owner**"* **[S-ST: §5]**; *"if that person … **sells Krytos**"* **[S-ST: F-11]** | **NO.** Art.3 says the *organisation* is owned by Krytos *under* the founder/CEO. **Whether the founder owns Krytos is not stated.** H-Q05 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-4** | **That Krytos and the founder are one actor** | `KRYTOS / Founder-CEO` as a single box **[S: Ch.4]**; *"ownership remains with **CEO/Krytos**"* **[§1.1]**; *"Owner: **Krytos / CEO**"* **[S: Ch.4 table]**; *"Prepared for **Krytos / Founder-CEO Vasu**"*; *"**Founder/Krytos-owned**"* **[S-E: N-E-17]**; *"a **Krytos / CEO-level** figure"* **[S-B: §8.7]** | **NO — and the opposite is also assumed** (C-5). **H-PD-7** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-5** | **That Krytos and the founder are two actors, with Krytos above** | **Diagram 3B**: `KRYTOS (Owner / product ecosystem)` **above** `Founder / CEO`; **[S-ST: §5]**: *"**Seven levels** (Krytos → Council → …)"* — Krytos counted as a **level** with latency; *"the entity's control follows **Krytos's own succession**, which is outside this architecture entirely"* — a succession distinct from the founder's | **NO.** Contradicts C-4 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-6** | **That Sewak Sathi is legally subordinate to Krytos** | The word *"**under**"* in *"legal structure **under** Krytos"* (11 occurrences across seven instruments); *"sits **inside** the broader Krytos ecosystem"*; *"a **component** owned by Krytos"* | **NO.** *"under"* is a preposition in a phrase whose object is undefined. **Art.3's ownership is *"subject to the final legal structure."*** §3.1 says only *"**not … an independent body that automatically supersedes the founder/CEO**"* — which denies automatic supersession, not independence | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-7** | **That Krytos can own another entity** | *"Separate company/**subsidiary**"* **[§3.2]**; *"**holding** structure"* **[S-ST: R4 (iii)]**; *"single vs. two-entity vs. **holding**"* **[S-ST: Annexure B]** | **NO.** H-Q13 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-8** | **That Krytos has money and may advance it** | *"**Krytos internal funding**"* **[S-B: §9]**; *"a source named in advance (founder capital, **Krytos**, or a validated donation)"* **[S-B: B-03]**; *"a central pool (donations, grants, founder capital, **Krytos**)"* **[S-B: §22.2]** | **NO.** No Krytos account, budget, capital or authority to advance is described. H-Q11, H-Q18 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-9** | **That Krytos holds, or can hold, the IP** | *"**Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution**; a licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | **NO.** The same row says IP is *"**Not addressed in v1.2**"* and *"**Absolute by default**"* — i.e. **by default, not by instrument.** H-Q09 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-10** | **That Krytos is, or is ultimately, the data fiduciary** | *"the legal data fiduciary is **presumably** the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | **NO** — and v1.3-A marks its own statement *"presumably."* **`A-N14` is `UNRESOLVED`** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED**, hedged by its own author |
| **C-11** | **That Krytos has governing documents that will operate on a succession event** | *"**Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]**"* **[S-A: A-14 S5]**; *"entity succession law, banking mandates, removal mechanisms, **Krytos governing documents**"* **[S-A: A-14 field 12]**; **A-V9** | **NO.** The documents are named, never produced. **OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** + **OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS** |
| **C-12** | **That Krytos has a risk profile capable of being mixed** | *"May **mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos**"* **[§3.2 row 1]** | **NO.** Mixing risk requires a risk-bearing person. H-Q01 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-13** | **That Krytos is a going concern with continuity** | *"the entity's control follows **Krytos's own succession**"* **[S-A: §11.2]**; *"**future owner of Krytos**"* **[S-ST: §1]**; *"**sells Krytos**"* **[S-ST: F-11]** | **NO.** H-Q12 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-14** | **That there is a "broader Krytos" with other operations** | *"**broader Krytos**"* **[§3.2 row 1]**; *"the **broader Krytos ecosystem**"* **[§3.1]**; *"the **wider organisation context**"* **[S-E: T2]** | **PARTLY** — four sibling projects are named **once** and never described. §I.5 finding 1 | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** as to content |
| **C-15** | **That naming Krytos in legal contexts will be possible and accurate** | **Rule E-58**: *"the **legal entity** is named accurately and the ownership relationship is stated without euphemism"*; **Rule E-59**: an accurate answer on request; **[S-E: §12]**: for a donor, *"**The legal entity, accurately named, with the Krytos relationship stated**"* | **NO.** There is no legal entity to name. **Rule E-58 is unexecutable today** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-16** | **That the phrase "under Krytos" constrains the entity choice in a determinate way** | *"**Entity form under Krytos**"* **[S-A: A-V1]**; *"Legal entity structure **under Krytos**"* **[S-F: SR-01]**; *"What is the legal entity structure **under Krytos?**"* **[S-D: OQ-05]** | **NO.** Counsel is asked to advise on a form *"under"* an undefined object. **This is the finding the whole instrument exists to state** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** |
| **C-17** | **That one person can decide the relationship** | *"Owner: **Founder/CEO**"* **[S-F: SR-01]**; *"Decision owner: **Founder/CEO** with corporate, tax and regulatory counsel"* **[S-ST: R4]**; **[S-F: Rule F-10]**: *"all three sit with the founder/CEO under [S: Ch.4] ownership authority"* | **PARTLY.** The founder can decide Sewak Sathi's structure. **Whether the founder can decide Krytos's — i.e. whether the founder controls Krytos — is C-3, which is undecided** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** for the Krytos side |
| **C-18** | **That the Krytos relationship is benign or at least neutral for the mission** | Art.3 is *"Not amended. **Not questioned**"* **[S-D: preservation table]**; *"That is a **legitimate design for a founder-led organisation** and v1.2 says so honestly"* **[S-A: §11.4]** | **CONTESTED BY THE CORPUS ITSELF** — **[S-ST: F-11]** is rated **CRITICAL** with *"blast radius **total**; reversibility **none**."* **The assumption and the finding coexist** | **SOURCE-ASSUMED** in v1.1/v1.2; **expressly rejected** in the stress test |

**[A] Model C, summarised:** **eighteen propositions, none decided, seventeen of them load-bearing.** The architecture uses Krytos as an owner, a parent, a funder, an IP holder, a data fiduciary, a brand tier, a jurisdiction of risk, a succession regime and a constraint on entity choice — **and establishes none of them.**

**[P] Rule H-10 (An Assumption Is Not Refuted By Being Named).** Identifying C-1…C-18 does not make them false. **Several may be true.** The finding is that **the architecture has never tested them**, and that **the four most load-bearing (C-1, C-3, C-6, C-11) are all facts about the world that one person can confirm or deny in a day.**

## II.4 MODEL D — WHAT REMAINS UNKNOWN

**[P]** **The gaps are not filled. They are listed.** Part III decomposes them into 28 questions; this model states them as categories.

| # | Category of the unknown | Corpus marker |
|---|---|---|
| **D-1** | **Whether Krytos is a legal person at all** | No occurrence of `incorporated`, `registered` (of Krytos), `certificate`, `PAN`, `deed`, `articles` (of Krytos) anywhere. `unincorporated` appears once, of a possible Sewak Sathi network **[S-C: HV-6]** |
| **D-2** | **Krytos's legal form, if any** | **[S-G: UE-24]**: *"No instrument records whether Krytos is a company, an LLP, a partnership, a proprietorship, an unregistered brand, a holding entity or a natural person trading"* |
| **D-3** | **Krytos's jurisdiction** | `jurisdiction` never predicated of Krytos |
| **D-4** | **Krytos's governing documents** | Named twice (**[S-A: A-14 S5]**, **A-V9**); **OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS** |
| **D-5** | **Who owns or controls Krytos** | C-3 assumed; never stated. *"**Whoever owns Krytos**"* **[S-A: §11.2]** is deliberately indefinite |
| **D-6** | **What Krytos owns** | No asset register. *"Property, brand, strategy"* **[S: Ch.4]** is the content of an authority type, not a schedule of assets |
| **D-7** | **Krytos's governance** | No body, no officers, no meetings, no resolutions, no cadence, no quorum, no removal route. **H-PD-5** |
| **D-8** | **Krytos's succession** | Named as existing (*"Krytos's own succession"*) and described as *"outside this architecture entirely."* **Content `UNKNOWN`** |
| **D-9** | **Krytos's financial position and authority** | No account, no capital, no budget, no auditor, no filing. **C-8 assumed** |
| **D-10** | **Krytos's IP position** | `A-N13` `UNRESOLVED`; *"Not addressed in v1.2"*; **C-9 assumed** |
| **D-11** | **Krytos's data role** | `A-N14` `UNRESOLVED`; **C-10 assumed with the word "presumably"** |
| **D-12** | **The four sibling projects** | Named once; form, activity, staff, assets, data, contracts, brand and relationship to Sewak Sathi all **`OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS`** |
| **D-13** | **Whether "under" denotes ownership, control, affiliation, licensure, hosting, sponsorship, or nothing legal at all** | **Six readings are available on the present text. None is excluded** |
| **D-14** | **Whether the v1.0 document decided any of this** | **OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS** (KP-40, KP-41, H-PD-1) |
| **D-15** | **Whether Krytos has ever acted** — signed anything, paid anything, employed anyone, held anything | No act of Krytos is recorded anywhere in the corpus. **Every recorded act is an act of the founder/CEO, a National function, or nobody** |

**[A] Model D, summarised:** **fifteen categories of the unknown, of which fourteen are closed by disclosure and one (D-14) by obtaining a document.** **Not one requires architectural work.**

# PART III — UE-24 DECOMPOSITION

## III.1 The item being decomposed

**[S-G: UE-24]** ⟨new — v1.3-G⟩, verbatim:

> *"**Krytos's own legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership.** The corpus describes Krytos only as *'parent/company context and ownership ecosystem'* **[S: Ch.1]** holding *'LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge and Trade Lab'* **[S: App. A §3.1]**. **Not stated anywhere.** No instrument records whether Krytos is a company, an LLP, a partnership, a proprietorship, an unregistered brand, a holding entity or a natural person trading. **[S-A: A-V9]** lists *'Krytos governing documents'* as a question for governance counsel — **which confirms the corpus knows the answer is missing.**"*
>
> **Blocks:** *"GC-02 … UE-01 for every option that presupposes a parent; UE-05/UE-06 (brand and IP may already sit in Krytos); UE-12 (whether Krytos may fund the centre); UE-16/UE-17 …; UE-07 …; related-party and transfer-pricing discipline."*
>
> **Decision:** *"**Founder/CEO** — the only person who holds this information. **[A]** This is a **disclosure** decision, not a legal one."*

**[A]** v1.3-G also recorded that UE-24 is *"**the cheapest of the four inputs that would move Part XXIV's verdict from B to A**."* **Part XVIII tests whether this instrument closes it.**

## III.2 The twenty-eight questions — H-Q01 … H-Q28

**Classification key:** **`ANSWERED`** = the corpus contains a source-established answer · **`PROPOSED`** = the corpus contains a proposal, not a decision · **`UNKNOWN`** = the corpus raises it and records no answer · **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** = the corpus records that the body which would answer is itself undetermined.

| ID | Question | Classification | **The corpus's position, exactly** | Consequence if unanswered |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H-Q01** | **What is Krytos?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Five descriptions exist and none is definitional: *"parent/company context and ownership ecosystem"* **[S: Ch.1]** · *"Owner / product ecosystem"* **[Diagram 3B]** · *"the broader Krytos ecosystem"* **[§3.1]** · *"T2 PARENT — Ownership and the wider organisation context"* **[S-E]** · *"broader Krytos"* **[§3.2 row 1]**. **`company` is never predicated of Krytos declaratively (§I.4)** | Every downstream question is unanswerable. **"Under Krytos" has no object** |
| **H-Q02** | **Is Krytos a legal person?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | `incorporated`, `registered`, `certificate`, `PAN`, `deed`, `articles` are never predicated of Krytos. **But C-1 is assumed throughout**: *"Krytos's governing documents"*, *"Krytos's own succession"*, *"subsidiary"*, *"division"*, *"whoever owns Krytos"* | Cannot hold assets, employ, contract, be sued, be a fiduciary, be a licensor, or be a parent — **and the architecture relies on at least six of those** |
| **H-Q03** | **If yes, what kind?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | No statement. **[S-G: UE-24]** lists seven possibilities and says the corpus records none. **H-PD-1**: the upstream document's own title is *"KRYTOS **ORGANISATION** MASTER ARCHITECTURE"* — *"organisation"* is not a legal form | **OPT-A…OPT-J of v1.3-G cannot be evaluated for any parent-based option** |
| **H-Q04** | **In what jurisdiction?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | `jurisdiction` is never predicated of Krytos. **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** warns of *"state-specific trust/society regimes"* and inter-state heterogeneity — **for Sewak Sathi, not for Krytos** | Objects, capacity, tax, filings, succession law and the availability of any group structure are all jurisdiction-specific |
| **H-Q05** | **Who legally controls Krytos?** | **`UNKNOWN`** — with a **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** answer | **C-3.** The corpus assumes one person: *"one person's ownership of the parent"* **[S-ST: F-11]**; *"a future owner of Krytos"*; *"under one private owner"* **[S-ST: §5]**; *"if that person … sells Krytos."* **But Art.3 says the *organisation* is owned by Krytos *under* the founder/CEO — which does not state that the founder owns Krytos.** *"**Whoever owns Krytos**"* **[S-A: §11.2]** is deliberately indefinite | **The whole of [S-ST: F-11] and [S-A: A-12] rests on this. If the founder does not control Krytos, the concentration analysis is wrong in one direction; if someone else does, it is wrong in a worse one** |
| **H-Q06** | **Who owns Krytos's assets?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | No Krytos asset is named anywhere. No asset register exists at any level | **Part VIII**; **UE-05, UE-06** |
| **H-Q07** | **Who appoints Krytos's governing authority?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Krytos has no described governing authority. **[S-D: Part 34]**'s 30-row authority map and **[S: App. E]**'s 20-row matrix contain **no Krytos row** (H-PD-5) | **H-Q07…H-Q08 are unanswerable in principle until H-Q01 is** |
| **H-Q08** | **Who appoints or removes Krytos's CEO?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Not addressed. **Note the contrast: Sewak Sathi's own CEO-appointment question is at least *registered* — as a reserved matter with `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` ([S-F: C-D-17]).** Krytos's is not registered at all | If Krytos's CEO changes, does Sewak Sathi's authority change? **Nobody has asked** |
| **H-Q09** | **Who owns Krytos's IP?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | *"**Not addressed in v1.2**"* · concentration **Absolute by default** · *"Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | **Part VIII**; **A-N13**; **UE-06** |
| **H-Q10** | **Who owns Krytos's brand — and does "Krytos" itself have a mark?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | *"The parent name 'Krytos'"* has a controller recorded as *"[S: Ch.4] ownership"* and a status of *"APPROVED as parent context"* **[S-E: Annexure F]** — **but no owner, no registration, no trade-mark class and no licence.** Contrast: the operating name is `UNRESOLVED (N12)`, `PROPOSED`, availability unvalidated | **NS-12.** The corpus treats the parent name as more settled than the operating name, and it is not |
| **H-Q11** | **Who controls Krytos's bank accounts?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | No Krytos account, mandate or signatory is described. **A-N16 concerns Sewak Sathi's mandate and is `UNRESOLVED`** | **H-Q18, H-Q19 and the whole of Part X** |
| **H-Q12** | **Who succeeds the founder *at Krytos*?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | *"**The entity's control follows Krytos's own succession, which is outside this architecture entirely**"* **[S-A: §11.2]** · *"Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]"* **[S-A: A-14 S5]** | **A succession regime is named as operative and its content is unknown. Part XII H-SX-03, H-SX-05** |

| **H-Q14** | **Can Krytos control another entity?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Presupposed by the word *"under"* and by Diagram 3B's ordering. **No control instrument — share, voting right, contractual right, board appointment right — is described** | **Part XIV**; **R2** |
| **H-Q15** | **Can Krytos license IP?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Presupposed by *"**a licensor could withdraw it**"* **[S-A: §11.2]** and by *"any licence to the operating entity"* **[S-A: N13]**. **No licence, no licensor and no licensee are identified** | **H-IP-04…H-IP-17**; **R6** |
| **H-Q16** | **Can Krytos provide services to Sewak Sathi?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Not addressed. **Note: the corpus assigns technology to a *National* department — *"Build and govern the internal operating system"* [S: App. A §4.2]** — not to Krytos. **The sibling project names (Code Forge, App Forge) suggest capability but no arrangement is described** | **R5**; **H-Q24**; **Part XIII H-IN-1** |
| **H-Q17** | **Can Sewak Sathi pay Krytos?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Not addressed. The waterfall's priority 5 is an *"**organisation** service fee"* — the recipient is not identified as Krytos or as anyone | **Part X H-MF-02**; related-party and transfer-pricing **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |
| **H-Q18** | **Can Krytos fund Sewak Sathi?** | **`PROPOSED`** — as a *candidate*, with the underlying authority `UNKNOWN` | *"founder capital, **Krytos internal funding**, donations, grants, or a pre-revenue service fee that is waived and accrued"* **[S-B: §9]**; *"a source named in advance (founder capital, **Krytos**, or a validated donation)"* **[S-B: B-03]**; **E-03** is `UNRESOLVED — no source instrument`; **[S-B: EV-4]** asks *"**Authority to advance funds**"* | **UE-12**; **GC-02**; **Part X H-MF-01**; **Part XIII H-IN-2** |
| **H-Q19** | **Can Krytos receive money from Sewak Sathi?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Not addressed. Priority 8 is *"**Residual surplus / founder upside**"* — which names a **founder**, not Krytos. **GC-01** | **Part VII**; **Part X H-MF-02** |
| **H-Q20** | **Can Krytos appoint Sewak Sathi leadership?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S: App. E]**: *"Leadership transfer — **CEO / governance process**."* **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**: appointment of a permanent CEO is a **reserved matter**. **[S-F: C-D-17]**: *"the CEO cannot appoint the CEO, and nobody else is determined."* **Krytos is never named as an appointing authority — but if Krytos owns the organisation, the question arises necessarily** | **Part XI**; **H-Q07**; **UE-25** |
| **H-Q21** | **Can Sewak Sathi operate independently of Krytos?** | **`UNKNOWN`** — with the corpus's only statement being a **qualified negative** | *"conceived as an operating organisation and product ecosystem component owned by Krytos, **not as an independent body that automatically supersedes the founder/CEO**"* **[§3.1]**. **[A]** The qualifier *"automatically"* and the object *"supersedes the founder/CEO"* mean this sentence denies **automatic supersession of a person**, not **operational independence from a context**. **It is routinely read as the latter (NS-13)** | **Part XIII in full** |
| **H-Q22** | **Can Sewak Sathi survive Krytos's dissolution?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Not addressed. **The reverse is addressed only obliquely: [S-ST: F-11]** *"If that person dies … or **sells Krytos** … nothing in the architecture prevents redirection of the institution"* | **Part XII H-SX-03**; **Art.19** |
| **H-Q23** | **Can Krytos survive Sewak Sathi's dissolution?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | Not addressed. **Art.19 covers Sewak Sathi's dissolution and says nothing about the parent** | **Part XII H-SX-04** |
| **H-Q24** | **Who controls shared infrastructure?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | The corpus assigns the platform to **National Technology** **[S: App. A §4.2]** and to an **AI & Data** department **[S: Ch.5]**. **A-14 §13.4** requires dual custody of *"platform, cloud, code repository, domain, payment gateway, communication channels, social accounts and **statutory portals**."* **No Krytos-provided infrastructure is described — and none is excluded** | **Part XIII H-IN-1**; **H-IP-07…H-IP-12** |
| **H-Q25** | **Who controls shared data?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | `A-N14` data fiduciary `UNRESOLVED`; *"**presumably** the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**"* **[S-A: §11.2]**; **A-N14's own action is *"Assign **per entity and per data category**"* — which contemplates more than one entity** | **Part IX in full** |
| **H-Q26** | **Who controls the Sewak Sathi brand?** | **`UNKNOWN`** — with **one `PROPOSED` assignment and one `UNRESOLVED` register entry in conflict** | **`PROPOSED`:** **[S: App. A §3.5]** assigns *"brand ownership"* to the **Founder/CEO**; **[S: Ch.4]** places *"Property, **brand**, strategy"* with *"Krytos / CEO."* **`UNRESOLVED`:** `A-N12` — *"Determine owner, licence terms, use conditions, post-exit restrictions"*; **[S-E: N-E-17]** offers four alternatives; **[S-E: BR-12]** — *"No rule in BR-1…BR-11 resolves them"*; **[S-F: XXIV.2]** — BR-9's *"grantor is undetermined."* **Note the identity of the two named holders in the source's own words: "Krytos / CEO" and "Founder/CEO" — H-PD-7** | **Part VIII**; **UE-05**; **GC-03**; **NS-12, NS-14** |

**Two further questions the decomposition requires, added because the source demands them:**

| ID | Question | Classification | Position |
|---|---|---|---|
| **H-Q27** ⟨added⟩ | **Who is the founder and CEO?** | **`ANSWERED`** | *"**Founder and CEO: Vasu**"* **[S-D: §27.1]**; *"founder/CEO **Vasu**"* **[S: Const. Art.3]**; *"Prepared for Krytos / Founder-CEO **Vasu**"* **[v1.1 front matter]**. **A single given name; no surname, no address, no office, no identification anywhere in the corpus.** **This is the only one of the twenty-seven questions the corpus answers** |
| **H-Q28** ⟨added⟩ | **Who may decide the Krytos relationship?** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** | **[S-F: SR-01]** assigns *"Legal entity structure under Krytos"* to the **Founder/CEO**. **But the founder's authority over Krytos is H-Q05, which is `UNKNOWN`.** If the founder does not control Krytos, the assigned decider cannot decide the relationship. **[S-F: Rule F-10]** applies with added force: *"the body that would decide them is itself partly constituted by them."* **Part XVII** |

## III.3 The decomposition result

| Classification | Count | Items |
|---|---|---|
| **`ANSWERED`** | **1** | H-Q27 |
| **`PROPOSED`** | **1** | H-Q18 *(as a funding candidate; its authority remains `UNKNOWN`)* |
| **`UNKNOWN`** | **25** | H-Q01…H-Q17 (17) and H-Q19…H-Q26 (8) |
| **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** | **1** | H-Q28 — *who may decide the Krytos relationship* |
| **`UNKNOWN` + `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` by dependency** | *(1, already counted above)* | H-Q20 — *can Krytos appoint Sewak Sathi leadership?* is `UNKNOWN` as to Krytos **and** `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` as to Sewak Sathi by **[S-F: C-D-17]** |
| **Total questions** | **28** | — |

**[A] Three findings:**

1. **One question in twenty-eight is answered, and it is the identity of a person.** *(Counts re-verified against the table above: 1 + 1 + 25 + 1 = 28.)* Not one question about Krytos's legal existence, form, jurisdiction, ownership, governance, assets, IP, data, banking or succession has an answer in 3.87 MB of architecture.

2. **The questions are not independent — they are a chain with one root.** **H-Q01 ("What is Krytos?") determines H-Q02…H-Q04; those determine H-Q05…H-Q15; those determine H-Q16…H-Q26.** **No question below H-Q04 can be answered before H-Q01 is.** This is the same structure v1.3-F found for `LDR-U08` (**[S-F: IV.4]**: *"the root"*), one level up.

3. **The chain has a single input, and it is not architectural.** **H-Q01 is answered by producing a document or by one person stating a fact.** **[S-G: UE-24]** already recorded that this is *"a **disclosure** decision, not a legal one"* and *"the cheapest of the four inputs that would move Part XXIV's verdict from B to A."* **Part XVIII confirms it is cheap and Part XXIII confirms it has not been made.**

**[P] Rule H-11 (The Decomposition Does Not Narrow `UE-24`; It Expands It).** v1.3-G framed UE-24 as *"Krytos's own legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership"* — **five facts.** This Part finds **twenty-eight questions**, of which **five are facts about Krytos's existence and identity** (H-Q01…H-Q04, H-Q06) and **the remaining twenty-three are about the *relationship*.** **`UE-24` was correctly identified and materially understated. H-GH-01 records the amendment required.**

# PART IV — RELATIONSHIP MODELS R1 … R8

**[P]** **Eight relationship models.** These are **relationship** models, distinct from Part II's four **identity** models. **Rule H-12 (Relationship Models Are Mutually Exclusive Except Where Marked):** exactly one of R1…R8 describes the true relationship. **R5 and R6 are not exclusive of R1–R4 and R7** — they are *overlay* relationships that may coexist with an ownership model; this is marked in each. **R8 is the current state of the corpus.**

## IV.1 The eight models

| ID | Model | One-sentence definition | Is it currently excluded? | Corpus position |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **INDEPENDENT** | Sewak Sathi Sanstha is a separate legal person with no ownership, control, contractual or financial relationship to Krytos; "Krytos" is a historical reference with no present legal effect | **NO — not excluded.** No instrument excludes it. **But it is in tension with A-4, A-5, A-6 and A-9** | **Not described anywhere** |
| **R2** | **PARENT / CONTROLLED (SUBSIDIARY)** | Krytos is a legal person that owns and controls Sewak Sathi Sanstha; Sewak Sathi is its subsidiary or division | **NO.** This is the **`SOURCE-ASSUMED` default** — Diagram 3B's ordering, *"under Krytos"*, *"subsidiary"*, *"division"*, *"component owned by Krytos"* | **Assumed throughout; never established** |
| **R3** | **COMMON CONTROL** | Krytos and Sewak Sathi are separate legal persons controlled by the same person(s) — neither owns the other | **NO.** Diagram 3B places `KRYTOS` and `Founder / CEO` in a chain that also permits this reading; *"Krytos / CEO"* **[S: Ch.4]** and *"CEO/Krytos"* **[§1.1]** are consistent with it | **Available on the text; never stated** |
| **R4** | **FOUNDER-LINKED BUT INDEPENDENT** | Sewak Sathi is independent in law; the founder is connected to both bodies personally, with no corporate link between them | **NO.** This is the reading most consistent with **§3.1's exact words** — *"not … an independent body that **automatically supersedes the founder/CEO**"* denies automatic supersession of a person, not independence from a context | **The reading the text best supports; never named** |
| **R5** | **SHARED SERVICES** *(overlay)* | One body provides services — technology, platform, administration, finance, HR — to the other under an arrangement, with or without an ownership link | **NO.** **Contested**: the corpus assigns the platform to **National Technology** **[S: App. A §4.2]** and to **AI & Data** **[S: Ch.5]**, not to Krytos; the sibling names *Code Forge* / *App Forge* suggest capability but no arrangement is described | **`UNKNOWN`** — H-Q16, H-Q24 |
| **R6** | **IP / LICENSING** *(overlay)* | One body owns the operating system, brand, marks, domain and content and licenses them to the other | **NO.** **Presupposed** by *"a licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** and *"any licence to the operating entity"* **[A-N13]** — but **no licensor and no licensee are identified** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED` in one sentence; otherwise `UNKNOWN`** |
| **R7** | **MULTI-ENTITY GROUP** | A group of legally distinct entities — Sewak Sathi plus LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge, Trade Lab and any others — under a common constitution, standards body and/or brand licence, with or without a single holding person | **NO.** Named in **two** places: *"the broader Krytos ecosystem … other Krytos projects such as LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge and Trade Lab"* **[§3.1]** and *"legally distinct state entities bound by a common constitution, standards body and brand licence"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** — the latter classified **LATER** by **[S-A: A-L7]** | **`PROPOSED` as a conception; `OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS` as to content** |
| **R8** | **OTHER / UNDEFINED** | The relationship is none of R1…R7, or is a combination not yet described, or **cannot be determined from the corpus** | — | **`UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`. This is the corpus's present position and the position this instrument certifies** |

## IV.2 What distinguishes R2, R3 and R4 — the three readings the corpus conflates

**[A]** This is the analytical core of Part IV. **Three of the eight models are routinely treated as one. They are not.** Each has a different legal consequence for Sewak Sathi, and each is supported by different sentences in the corpus.

| Test | **R2 — Parent / Controlled** | **R3 — Common Control** | **R4 — Founder-linked but independent** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Does Krytos own Sewak Sathi?** | **Yes** | **No** | **No** |
| **Can Krytos appoint or remove Sewak Sathi's CEO?** | **Depends on Krytos's instrument** — possibly yes | **No**, unless separately contracted | **No** |
| **Do Sewak Sathi's assets belong to Krytos?** | **Possibly** — depends on form | **No** | **No** |
| **Is Sewak Sathi's brand Krytos's property?** | **Possibly** — **[S: Ch.4]** *"Property, brand, strategy"* is held by *"Krytos / CEO"* | **No** | **No** |
| **Is money from Krytos to Sewak Sathi a related-party transaction?** | **Yes** | **Yes** | **No** |
| **Is a licence needed for the operating system?** | **Yes** — and a licensor *"could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | **Yes** | **Yes, if the IP sits in Krytos** |
| **Does Krytos's insolvency affect Sewak Sathi?** | **Yes** | **No**, except by contract | **No** |
| **Does selling Krytos transfer Sewak Sathi?** | **Yes** — the **[S-ST: F-11]** scenario | **No** | **No** |
| **Does the founder's death change Sewak Sathi's control?** | **Yes — twice**: entity succession law **and** Krytos's governing documents *"in parallel"* **[S-A: A-14 S5]** | **Yes — twice**, by the same mechanism | **Yes — once**: entity succession law only |
| **Can a mission lock bind the ultimate owner?** | **No, absent an instrument** — *"not an unrelated owner of the parent unless instrumented"* **[S-A: A-13 §12.2]** | **No**, unless the common controller agrees | **Yes**, because the entity's own governing document is the top instrument |
| **Which corpus sentences support it?** | Art.3; §1.1 rows 1–3; §2.1; Diagram 3B; §3.2 rows 1–2; §3.3; Ch.1 chain; Ch.4; §6; S-E T2/Rule E-58; S-ST §5 | §1.1 *"ownership remains with **CEO/Krytos**"*; Ch.4 *"**Krytos / CEO**"* and *"Krytos / Founder-CEO"*; §3.5 *"Founder/CEO — brand ownership"*; v1.1 front matter *"Prepared for **Krytos / Founder-CEO Vasu**"* | §3.1's exact words (*"**not … automatically** supersedes"*); A-9's qualifier; the absence of any Krytos instrument; §3.2's four options of which **two create no corporate link at all**; **[S-E: Rule E-58]**'s requirement that the *legal entity* be named accurately |
| **Which corpus sentences contradict it?** | §3.2 — *"Separate company/subsidiary"* and *"Nonprofit entity/trust/society/Section 8"* are **undecided alternatives**, so R2 is **one of four**, not the position; Art.3 is *"subject to the final legal structure"* | The ordering of Diagram 3B (`KRYTOS` above `Founder / CEO`) is inconsistent with a peer relationship; **[S-ST: §5]** counts Krytos as a **level** above the Council | Art.3 read plainly (*"owned by Krytos"*); §2.1 (*"remains owned by Krytos"*); §1.1 row 1 (*"Preserved as APPROVED ARCHITECTURE"*) |
| **Status** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** — available on the text, never named | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** — best supported by §3.1's exact words, never named |

**[P] Rule H-13 (No Default Model).** **R2 is not the default merely because it appears most often.** It appears most often because it was **preserved from an upstream document that is not in the corpus** (**[S: App. A §1.1]**: *"Preserved as APPROVED ARCHITECTURE"*; KP-40). **Repetition across eight instruments that all inherit one unsupplied source is one occurrence, not eight (Rule H-3).**

**[A] Finding H-PD-11 (new).** **The corpus never distinguishes R2, R3 and R4, and the distinction is the single most consequential undecided fact in the architecture.** Under R2 a sale of Krytos transfers the institution; under R3 it does not; under R4 it cannot. **[S-ST: F-11]** — rated **CRITICAL**, *"blast radius total; reversibility none"* — **is written as though R2 were settled.** If the true model is R4, F-11's mechanism does not exist. **If the true model is R2, F-11 is the most important finding in the entire corpus and no mitigation has been adopted.** **Either way, the corpus cannot presently say which.**

## IV.3 The overlay models R5 and R6

**[P]** **R5 and R6 may coexist with R1, R2, R3, R4 or R7.** They are therefore recorded separately, and each combination is a distinct legal fact pattern.

| Combination | What it would mean | Corpus support | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1 + R5** | Independent bodies with a services agreement | None | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **R1 + R6** | Independent bodies with an IP licence — **the arrangement [S-A: §11.2] contemplates when it says *"a licensor could withdraw it"*** | Indirect only | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **R2 + R5** | Parent providing services to a subsidiary — **intra-group, related-party** | *"Internal Krytos division"* **[§3.2 row 1]** implies the extreme of this | **`PROPOSED`** |
| **R2 + R6** | Parent licensing brand and IP to a subsidiary — **the hybrid [S-ST: R4 (ii)] contemplates** | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**: *"a brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* | **`PROPOSED`** `[V]` |
| **R3 + R5** / **R3 + R6** | Commonly controlled bodies transacting with each other — **the transfer-pricing case in its purest form** | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | **`PROPOSED`** `[V]` |
| **R4 + R6** | Independent entity licensing IP from a founder-linked holder — **requires an arm's-length licence with real terms** | None | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **R7 + R5 + R6** | A group with shared services and a common brand licence — **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** at scale | *"a common constitution, standards body and brand licence"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** | **`PROPOSED`** `[V]`; classified **LATER** by **[S-A: A-L7]** |
| **R8 + any** | Undefined | — | **The present state** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-12 (new).** **No combination in this table is chosen, and the choice is not recorded as pending.** The corpus's own nearest approach is **[S-ST: Annexure B]** — *"single vs. two-entity vs. holding"* — which is a **count** of entities, not a **relationship** between them. **Counting entities does not answer R1…R8.**
