# PART V — CONSEQUENCE MATRIX: 8 MODELS × 30 DIMENSIONS

## V.0 Reading the matrix

**[P]** **240 cells.** Each cell carries a **status** and, where the corpus supplies one, a **provenance marker**. Where the corpus supplies none, the cell says so.

**Cell status key — the five labels the brief requires:**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** | The corpus contains an explicit statement supporting the cell's consequence, at a stated ID |
| **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | The corpus contains a proposal, option or recommendation supporting the cell — **which has no operative force ([S-F: Rule F-9])** |
| **`UNKNOWN`** | The corpus raises the dimension and records no answer, or does not raise it at all |
| **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | The cell cannot be completed without counsel, a CA, a CS, a broker, an IP attorney or a data-protection professional |
| **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** | The cell's consequence is determined by another cell or another model choice and cannot be evaluated independently. **The dependency is named in the cell** |

**[P] Rule H-14 (No Cell Is Filled By Analogy).** A consequence that would be true of a company is not recorded as true of Krytos. **A cell may be `UNKNOWN` because the corpus is silent about Krytos even where the general law is not silent.** Where general professional knowledge would supply an answer, the cell says **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** and names nothing.

**[P] Rule H-15 (The `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION` Marker).** Where a cell would require a **yes/no** judgement about whether a structure works, the cell carries **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** instead. **This instrument renders no such verdict (v1.3-G Rule G-1 adopted).**

**The thirty dimensions:**

| # | Dimension | # | Dimension |
|---|---|---|---|
| **HD-01** | Legal personality of Sewak Sathi | **HD-16** | Money flows out of Sewak Sathi |
| **HD-02** | Who is the legal owner of the institution | **HD-17** | Related-party transactions and transfer pricing |
| **HD-03** | Entity formation requirement | **HD-18** | Employment and worker liability |
| **HD-04** | Registration, licences and statutory compliance | **HD-19** | Contracts with third parties |
| **HD-05** | Tax status and treatment | **HD-20** | Insurance |
| **HD-06** | Governance authority above the National Council | **HD-21** | Donations, grants and donor disclosure |
| **HD-07** | Appointment and removal of the CEO | **HD-22** | Public representation and the no-euphemism rule |
| **HD-08** | Reserved matters — who holds them | **HD-23** | Member and volunteer rights and liability |
| **HD-09** | Ownership of tangible assets | **HD-24** | Dissolution and asset transfer |
| **HD-10** | Ownership of the operating-system IP | **HD-25** | Succession on founder death or incapacity |
| **HD-11** | Ownership of the brand and marks | **HD-26** | Enforceability of a mission lock |
| **HD-12** | Data fiduciary role | **HD-27** | Pilot authorisation |
| **HD-13** | Data sharing between the two bodies | **HD-28** | Audit, accounts and statutory filings |
| **HD-14** | Banking — accounts and mandates | **HD-29** | Dispute resolution and forum |
| **HD-15** | Funding flows into Sewak Sathi | **HD-30** | Scale ceiling and structural headroom |

## V.1 R1 — INDEPENDENT

**[P]** *Premise: Sewak Sathi Sanstha is a separate legal person; Krytos has no ownership, control, contractual or financial relationship to it.*

| # | Dimension | Consequence under R1 | Cell status | Provenance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HD-01 | Legal personality | Sewak Sathi **must be a legal person in its own right**; nothing else in R1 is possible without it. **[S-F: P0-A]** *"the entity exists"* becomes the first requirement rather than a pilot blocker | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on OPT-A…OPT-J of v1.3-G — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-F: P0-A]**; **[S-G: Part III]** |
| HD-02 | Legal owner | **No owner above the entity.** Ownership is whatever the chosen form provides — members, trustees, settlors or none. The sentence *"owned by Krytos"* would have to be **withdrawn, not reinterpreted** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the conflict | **[S: Const. Art.3]**; **[S-D: preservation table]** *"Not amended. Not questioned"* |
| HD-03 | Entity formation | **Mandatory and unqualified.** GC-02's *"internal division"* route is closed; only OPT-A…OPT-J excluding a Krytos division remain | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-G: GC-02]**; **[S: App. A §3.2]** |
| HD-04 | Registration & compliance | **Sewak Sathi's own registrations only.** No group filing, no consolidated return, no parent disclosure — **subject to counsel** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-05 | Tax status | **Assessed on Sewak Sathi alone.** No group relief, no set-off against Krytos losses, no consolidation | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* | — |
| HD-06 | Authority above the Council | **None.** The National Council becomes the top internal body, and **`LDR-U01` (CEO vs National Council) remains exactly as unresolved as it is today** — R1 does not fix it | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** | **[S: App. A §1.2]**; **[S-G: UE-01]** |
| HD-07 | CEO appointment / removal | **By the entity's own instrument.** **[S-F: C-D-17]** stays open — R1 removes a candidate answer (Krytos) rather than supplying one | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`* | **[S-F: C-D-17]** |
| HD-08 | Reserved matters | **Held inside the entity**, by a body that **[S-F: Rule F-10]** shows is partly constituted by them. **No external holder exists in R1** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to content; **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** as to holder | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-D: LDR-C26]** |
| HD-09 | Tangible assets | **Sewak Sathi's own.** No parent claim, no group asset pool | **`UNKNOWN`** — no asset register exists at any level | **[S-G: UE-05]** |
| HD-10 | Operating-system IP | **Either the entity owns it, or it must be licensed in from somewhere — and in R1 the licensor cannot be a related body.** *"A licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** becomes an **arm's-length** risk | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-A: §11.2]**; `A-N13` |
| HD-11 | Brand & marks | **Entity-owned.** **[S-E: N-E-17]**'s alternative *"entity-owned"* becomes the only one consistent with R1; *"Founder/Krytos-owned"* is excluded | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** — one of four alternatives | **[S-E: N-E-17]**; `A-N12` |
| HD-12 | Data fiduciary | **Sewak Sathi alone.** **`A-N14`'s hedge — *"presumably the entity, i.e. ultimately Krytos"* — resolves to its first half and its second half is deleted** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-A: §11.2]**; `A-N14` |
| HD-13 | Data sharing | **No intra-group sharing basis exists.** Any sharing with Krytos or the four sibling projects is a **third-party disclosure** requiring its own lawful basis | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| HD-14 | Banking | **Sewak Sathi's own mandate.** **A-N16 remains a BLOCKER** and R1 does not close it | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the blocker | **[S-A: A-N16]**; **[S-G: Part XIX PV-01…PV-09]** |
| HD-15 | Money in | **Krytos cannot fund the centre as a related body.** *"Krytos internal funding"* **[S-B: §9]** is removed from the candidate list; E-03 becomes **harder**, not easier | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the candidate list | **[S-B: §9, E-03, B-03]** |
| HD-16 | Money out | **No distribution to any external owner.** Priority 8 *"Residual surplus / founder upside"* **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** loses its recipient and **GC-01 sharpens** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-01 | **[S-G: GC-01]** |
| HD-17 | Related-party / transfer pricing | **None by definition** — unless the founder personally transacts with the entity, which R1 does not exclude | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |
| HD-18 | Employment | **Sewak Sathi is the sole employer.** No secondment, no shared payroll, no dual employment | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-G: Part X]** |
| HD-19 | Third-party contracts | **Sewak Sathi signs and is liable.** **Rule E-58 is fully executable for the first time** — the legal entity is named accurately and *"the ownership relationship"* is stated as *"none"* | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-58, E-59]** |
| HD-20 | Insurance | **Placed on Sewak Sathi alone.** No group programme, no parent's cover to rely on — **and per v1.3-G this remains a QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL, never "COVERAGE EXISTS"** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`* | **[S-G: Part XVI INS-01…INS-12]** |
| HD-21 | Donations & donor disclosure | **The donor is told the entity's name and that there is no owner.** *"The legal entity, accurately named, with the Krytos relationship stated"* **[S-E: §12]** becomes *"…with no Krytos relationship"* | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to the disclosure rule | **[S-E: §12, Rule E-59]** |
| HD-22 | Public representation | **Fully determinate.** Rule E-59's *"accurate answer in plain language"* is *"this organisation is independent"* | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |
| HD-23 | Members & volunteers | **Rights and obligations run to Sewak Sathi only.** No parent's terms, no group code of conduct | **`UNKNOWN`** — no member instrument exists | **[S-G: UE-08…UE-11]** |
| HD-24 | Dissolution | **Art.19 operates alone.** No parent claim on residual assets; no upstream transfer | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to Art.19 | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |
| HD-25 | Succession | **One regime, not two.** *"Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]"* **[S-A: A-14 S5]** **ceases to apply.** The founder's death affects the entity through entity succession law only | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the parallel-regime statement | **[S-A: A-14 S5]** |
| HD-26 | Mission lock | **Fully enforceable at the top.** *"An irrevocable purpose clause in the governing document of the operating entity"* **[S-ST: F-11 (i)]** binds everything, because there is nothing above | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-ST: F-11 item 7]**; **[S-A: A-13]** |
| HD-27 | Pilot authorisation | **Unblocked by the relationship question.** GC-02's *"internal-division pilot"* route is closed; only an entity-based pilot remains | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-G: GC-02]** |
| HD-28 | Audit & filings | **Sewak Sathi's own.** No consolidated accounts | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-G: UE-27]** |
| HD-29 | Disputes & forum | **One party.** No intra-group dispute mechanism needed | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |
| HD-30 | Scale ceiling | **Set by the chosen form alone.** **[S-ST: §5 Scenario E]**'s *"no legal form comfortably holds a 10M-member public institution under one private owner"* **does not apply**, because there is no private owner | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the limit's condition | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario E]** |

**[A] R1 summary:** **R1 is the only model in which every one of the thirty dimensions becomes determinate without an unknown Krytos.** It achieves this by **deleting** the architecture's most repeated proposition. **R1 is not recommended and not excluded. It is recorded as the model with the fewest unknowns and the largest amendment burden — [S-D: preservation table] would have to change.**

## V.3 R3 — COMMON CONTROL

**[P]** *Premise: Krytos and Sewak Sathi are separate legal persons controlled by the same person(s); neither owns the other.*

| # | Dimension | Consequence under R3 | Cell status | Provenance |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| HD-02 | Legal owner | **Neither owns the other.** *"Owned by Krytos"* **[Art.3]** would be **incorrect as drafted** and would require amendment | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the conflict | **[S: Const. Art.3]** |
| HD-03 | Entity formation | **Mandatory for Sewak Sathi**, and separately for Krytos if it is not already formed | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on H-Q02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | — |
| HD-04 | Registration & compliance | **Two sets.** Any group-level obligation depends on Krytos's form and jurisdiction — **`UNKNOWN`** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | H-Q03, H-Q04 |
| HD-05 | Tax status | **Assessed separately, with related-party rules between them.** **Whether they are "related" without an ownership link is a professional question** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* | — |
| HD-06 | Authority above the Council | **None from Krytos.** The common controller acts **personally**, not through Krytos — so `LDR-U01` remains unresolved, and Diagram 3B's ordering is wrong | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the conflict with Diagram 3B | **[S: App. A §3.3]**; **[S: App. A §1.2]** |
| HD-07 | CEO appointment / removal | **Through the common controller's rights in Sewak Sathi only** — which depend on Sewak Sathi's form. **`C-D-17` stays open** | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`* | **[S-F: C-D-17]** |
| HD-08 | Reserved matters | **Held inside Sewak Sathi**, but exercisable by a person who also controls another body — **a conflict-of-interest dimension no instrument addresses** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to content; **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** as to holder | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** |

| HD-10 | Operating-system IP | **Wherever it sits, a licence is required if the other body uses it.** *"A licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** applies at arm's length between related persons | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| HD-11 | Brand & marks | **Separate.** *"Founder/Krytos-owned"* **[S-E: N-E-17]** would mean founder-owned with Krytos as a peer user | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-E: N-E-17]** |
| HD-12 | Data fiduciary | **Sewak Sathi alone.** *"Ultimately Krytos"* **[S-A: §11.2]** would be **wrong** — there is no upstream controller in R3 | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the conflict | **[S-A: §11.2]**; `A-N14` |
| HD-13 | Data sharing | **Between two separate controllers.** The strictest of the eight models for data — no intra-group basis is available | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-A: A-N14]** |

| HD-15 | Money in | **From Krytos only as a third-party advance or donation** — with the same `UNRESOLVED — no source instrument` status | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as candidate | **[S-B: §9, E-03]** |
| HD-16 | Money out | **No entitlement.** Any payment to Krytos is a **contractual** payment requiring an agreement that does not exist | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S-G: GC-01]** |
| HD-17 | Related-party / transfer pricing | **Applies in full.** Common control is the classic case; *"strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |
| HD-18 | Employment | **Separate employers.** Dual employment or secondment requires an instrument — **none exists** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-19 | Third-party contracts | **Sewak Sathi signs and is liable.** **Rule E-58 executable**: the entity is named accurately and the relationship stated as *"separately controlled by the same founder"* | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-58]** |
| HD-20 | Insurance | **Separate programmes.** Cross-liability between commonly controlled bodies is a broker question | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`* | **[S-G: Part XVI]** |
| HD-21 | Donations & donor disclosure | **The relationship must still be disclosed** — a donor giving to Sewak Sathi is giving to a body whose controller also runs four other projects | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to the disclosure rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |

| HD-23 | Members & volunteers | **Sewak Sathi's own.** No parent's terms apply | **`UNKNOWN`** — no member instrument | **[S-G: UE-08…UE-11]** |
| HD-24 | Dissolution | **Art.19 alone.** Krytos has no claim | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to Art.19 | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |
| HD-25 | Succession | **Two successions, but not nested.** The common controller's death affects both bodies through their own instruments — **no *"parallel"* Krytos regime operates on Sewak Sathi's control** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the distinction from R2 | **[S-A: A-14 S5]** |
| HD-26 | Mission lock | **Binds Sewak Sathi; does not bind Krytos.** But because Krytos does not own Sewak Sathi, **the F-11 sale mechanism does not transfer the institution** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-ST: F-11]**; **[S-A: A-13]** |
| HD-27 | Pilot authorisation | **Requires an entity.** No division route exists in R3 | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-G: GC-02]** |
| HD-28 | Audit & filings | **Separate**, with related-party disclosure between them | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-29 | Disputes & forum | **Between two related bodies with no dispute mechanism.** **`UNKNOWN`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |
| HD-30 | Scale ceiling | **Higher than R2's**, because there is no single-owner ceiling. **The corpus's ~1M limit is conditioned on *"one private owner"* and would not apply** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the limit's condition | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario E]** |

**[A] R3 summary:** **R3 is the model the corpus's own ownership language most often implies and never names.** *"Ownership remains with CEO/Krytos"* **[§1.1]**, *"Owner: Krytos / CEO"* **[S: Ch.4]**, *"Prepared for Krytos / Founder-CEO Vasu"* **[v1.1 front matter]** and *"Founder/Krytos-owned"* **[S-E: N-E-17]** all treat the two as **one holder expressed two ways** — which is common control, not parentage. **R3 removes F-11's transfer mechanism while keeping its concentration problem.**

| HD-16 | Money out | **To the founder personally, if the form permits — and GC-01 becomes a *personal* conflict of interest rather than a group one.** *"Restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"* **[§3.2 row 3]** is engaged directly | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the conflict — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-G: GC-01]**; **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** |
| HD-17 | Related-party / transfer pricing | **Applies to founder–entity dealings** even without a corporate link | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |
| HD-18 | Employment | **The entity is the sole employer.** The founder may be an employee, an officer or a contractor — **undetermined** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-G: Part X]** |
| HD-19 | Third-party contracts | **The entity signs and is liable. Rule E-58 fully executable** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-58]** |
| HD-20 | Insurance | **The entity's own programme.** Key-person exposure on the founder is a broker question — **QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`* | **[S-G: Part XVI]** |
| HD-21 | Donations & donor disclosure | **The founder's other interests must be disclosed as a conflict**, not as ownership | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to the disclosure rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |
| HD-22 | Public representation | **Determinate.** *"An independent organisation founded by Vasu, who is also associated with Krytos."* | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |
| HD-23 | Members & volunteers | **The entity's own** | **`UNKNOWN`** — no member instrument | **[S-G: UE-08…UE-11]** |
| HD-24 | Dissolution | **Art.19 alone.** No upstream claim | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to Art.19 | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |
| HD-25 | Succession | **One regime.** The founder's death affects the entity through entity succession law; **Krytos's documents do not operate on the entity at all** — because there is no link for them to travel through | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the distinction from R2 | **[S-A: A-14 S5]** |
| HD-26 | Mission lock | **Fully enforceable at the top, as R1.** **F-11's sale mechanism does not exist** — selling Krytos does not transfer Sewak Sathi | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-ST: F-11]**; **[S-A: A-13]** |
| HD-27 | Pilot authorisation | **Requires an entity** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-G: GC-02]** |
| HD-28 | Audit & filings | **The entity's own** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-29 | Disputes & forum | **Founder–entity disputes have no mechanism.** **This is R4's characteristic gap: the founder is on both sides of every conflict and no instrument addresses it** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |
| HD-30 | Scale ceiling | **Set by the entity's form alone**, as R1 | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the limit's condition | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario E]** |

**[A] R4 summary:** **R4 is the reading §3.1's exact words best support and the reading no instrument names.** It preserves the founder's personal centrality — which is what the architecture actually wants — **without making the institution the property of an undocumented body.** Its characteristic risk is not F-11 but **HD-29**: a founder on both sides of every transaction with no conflict mechanism.

## V.5 R5 — SHARED SERVICES *(overlay)*

**[P]** *Premise: one body provides services to the other under an arrangement. **R5 does not determine ownership; it operates on top of R1, R2, R3, R4 or R7.** Cells that R5 cannot determine are marked `STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT` and name the underlying model.*

| # | Dimension | Consequence under R5 | Cell status | Provenance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HD-01 | Legal personality | **Both service provider and recipient must be legal persons to contract.** If Sewak Sathi is a Krytos division, **there is no service relationship — only internal allocation** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on R2/GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S: App. A §3.2]** |
| HD-02 | Legal owner | **Not determined by R5** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on R1/R2/R3/R4 | — |
| HD-03 | Entity formation | **Required for both contracting parties** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-F: P0-A]** |
| HD-04 | Registration & compliance | **Service provision may itself be a regulated activity** — e.g. payment processing, data processing, recruitment | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-05 | Tax status | **A services fee is taxable income to the provider and an expense to the recipient.** **GC-01's *"organisation service fee"* at priority 5 has no identified recipient** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-G: GC-01]** |
| HD-06 | Authority above the Council | **Not determined by R5.** But a services provider with control of the platform has **de facto** authority the architecture does not acknowledge | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`* | **[S-A: §11.2]**; H-Q24 |
| HD-07 | CEO appointment / removal | **Not determined by R5** | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`* | **[S-F: C-D-17]** |
| HD-08 | Reserved matters | **Not determined by R5** | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`* | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** |
| HD-09 | Tangible assets | **Equipment supplied under a service arrangement may remain the provider's property** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-G: UE-05]** |
| HD-10 | Operating-system IP | **The central R5 cell.** The corpus assigns *"Build and govern the internal operating system"* to **National Technology** **[S: App. A §4.2]** — **i.e. inside Sewak Sathi, not in Krytos.** R5 would move it out; nothing in the corpus does | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the current assignment | **[S: App. A §4.2]**; **[S: Ch.5]** |
| HD-11 | Brand & marks | **Not determined by R5**, but a shared-services provider often supplies brand guidelines — **which is what [S-E: Annexure F] attempts, with the parent name approved and every operating mark `UNRESOLVED`** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-E: Annexure F]** |
| HD-12 | Data fiduciary | **A service provider handling member data is a processor or a joint fiduciary — which is exactly the distinction `A-N14` does not draw.** *"Assign per entity and per data category"* **[A-N14]** anticipates it and supplies nothing | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-A: A-N14]** |
| HD-13 | Data sharing | **A data-processing agreement is required and does not exist** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-A: A-N14]** |
| HD-14 | Banking | **Service fees require a payment route between the two bodies — which requires both to have accounts. Krytos's is `UNKNOWN` (H-Q11)** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S-A: A-N16]** |
| HD-15 | Money in | **Not applicable** — R5 moves money **out** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on HD-16 | — |
| HD-16 | Money out | **Service fees.** Priority 5's *"organisation service fee"* **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** is the corpus's only such flow and **its recipient is unnamed** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the flow; **`UNKNOWN`** as to the recipient | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-G: GC-01]** |
| HD-17 | Related-party / transfer pricing | **Fully engaged** — a services arrangement between related bodies is the archetypal transfer-pricing case | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |
| HD-18 | Employment | **Secondment and dual employment require instruments that do not exist** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-19 | Third-party contracts | **The service agreement itself is the missing contract.** Rule E-58 requires the legal entity to be named in contractual contexts — **unexecutable today (C-15)** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-58]** |
| HD-20 | Insurance | **Professional indemnity, cyber and errors-and-omissions exposure follows the service provider.** **QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`* | **[S-G: Part XVI]** |
| HD-21 | Donations & donor disclosure | **A donor paying for services delivered by a third body must be told** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to the disclosure rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |
| HD-22 | Public representation | **The service relationship is disclosable and currently undisclosed** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |
| HD-23 | Members & volunteers | **Members' data and service experience pass through a body they never contracted with** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-24 | Dissolution | **Service arrangements must be unwound; assets supplied must be returned or purchased** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |
| HD-25 | Succession | **A services contract survives the founder if properly made; an informal arrangement does not** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-26 | Mission lock | **A service provider can defeat a mission lock operationally without touching it legally** — by withdrawing the platform. *"A licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** | **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| HD-27 | Pilot authorisation | **A pilot cannot run without the services it depends on** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on HD-10, HD-14 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | — |
| HD-28 | Audit & filings | **Service fees are auditable transactions requiring documentation that does not exist** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-29 | Disputes & forum | **Service disputes have no mechanism** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |
| HD-30 | Scale ceiling | **Set by the provider's capacity, not by ownership** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |

**[A] R5 summary:** **R5 is the model the corpus comes closest to describing without ever naming — and the closest it comes is to *deny* it, by assigning the operating system to a National department inside Sewak Sathi ([S: App. A §4.2]).** **The four sibling projects (Code Forge, App Forge) named once at §3.1 are the only hint of external service capability, and they are described nowhere.**

**[P]** *Premise: one body owns the operating system, brand, marks, domain and content and licenses them to the other.*

| HD-02 | Legal owner | **Not determined by R6** — but **R6 is the model in which *"ownership"* of the institution's operating system is separated from ownership of the institution** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on R1/R2/R3/R4 | **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| HD-03 | Entity formation | **Required for both parties** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on GC-02 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-F: P0-A]** |

| HD-12 | Data fiduciary | **Not determined by R6**, but licensed software may carry data-processing consequences | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | `A-N14` |
| HD-13 | Data sharing | **Not determined by R6** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |

| HD-16 | Money out | **Royalties.** The waterfall contains **no royalty line** — priorities 1…8 are operational costs, compensation, reserves, an *"organisation service fee"* and residual surplus | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the absence | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-G: GC-01]** |
| HD-17 | Related-party / transfer pricing | **Royalty pricing between related bodies is a transfer-pricing question in its purest form** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |

| HD-24 | Dissolution | **The licence ends; the entity retains nothing it did not own.** *"Post-exit restrictions"* **[A-N12]** is the corpus's only reference to this and is `UNRESOLVED` | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the item's existence | **[S-A: A-N12]** |
| HD-25 | Succession | **A licence may or may not survive the licensor's death — depending on terms that do not exist.** *"Krytos's own succession"* **[S-A: §11.2]** would determine it | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| HD-26 | Mission lock | **Defeatable by withdrawal.** *"A licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** — **the corpus's own statement that a mission lock resting on licensed IP is not a lock** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** | **[S-A: §11.2]**; **[S-A: A-13 §12.4]** |
| HD-27 | Pilot authorisation | **A pilot cannot lawfully use unlicensed IP** | **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`** — on HD-10, HD-11 — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | — |
| HD-28 | Audit & filings | **Intangible assets and royalty expense must be accounted for; nothing exists to account** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-29 | Disputes & forum | **Licence disputes have no mechanism** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |
| HD-30 | Scale ceiling | **Set by the licence's scope, not by ownership** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |

**[A] R6 summary:** **R6 is the only model the corpus states as an operative risk — *"a licensor could withdraw it"* — while leaving every one of its terms undefined.** **It is also the model in which [S-ST: F-11]'s mitigation (a mission lock) is most fragile, because the lock cannot bind a licensor who is outside the entity.**

## V.7 R7 — MULTI-ENTITY GROUP

**[P]** *Premise: a group of legally distinct entities — Sewak Sathi plus LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge, Trade Lab and any others — under a common constitution, standards body and/or brand licence.*

| # | Dimension | Consequence under R7 | Cell status | Provenance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HD-01 | Legal personality | **Every entity in the group must be a legal person.** **Four of the five named projects are described nowhere in the corpus** | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S: App. A §3.1]**; §I.5 finding 1 |
| HD-02 | Legal owner | **A group needs a holder.** *"Holding structure with the mission held separately from both"* **[S-ST: R4 (iii)]** is `PROPOSED` `[V]`; *"a common constitution, standards body and brand licence"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-ST: R4 (iii), §5 Scenario D]** |
| HD-03 | Entity formation | **Multiple formations.** The corpus contemplates at scale *"legally distinct state entities"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** — classified **LATER** by **[S-A: A-L7]** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`**; **timing `LATER`** — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-A: A-L7]** |
| HD-04 | Registration & compliance | **N registrations × M jurisdictions.** *"State-specific trust/society regimes"* **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** is the corpus's own warning | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** |
| HD-05 | Tax status | **Group taxation, cross-charging and consolidation are all professional questions of the highest order** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | — |
| HD-06 | Authority above the Council | **A group body above every entity's council.** **[S-ST: §5 item 1]** counts **seven levels** with Krytos at the top and latency at each | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the count | **[S-ST: §5 item 1]** |
| HD-07 | CEO appointment / removal | **By the group body, if one exists.** **No group body is described** | **`UNKNOWN`** — *`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`* | H-Q07, H-Q08 |
| HD-08 | Reserved matters | **Split between entity and group.** *"Change of mission, name, brand or entity structure"* **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** would sit at group level; the corpus does not say | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to content; **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** as to holder — *`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`* | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** |
| HD-09 | Tangible assets | **Per entity, with shared-use agreements that do not exist** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S-G: UE-05]** |
| HD-10 | Operating-system IP | **Group-held and licensed to each entity — the [S-ST: §5 Scenario D] *"brand licence"* model extended to the operating system.** **Nothing in the corpus places the operating system outside Sewak Sathi** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]**; **[S: App. A §4.2]** |
| HD-11 | Brand & marks | **Group-held with a common standard.** **This is what [S-E: Annexure F] half-builds**: parent name `APPROVED as parent context`, every operating mark `UNRESOLVED` | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** + `UNRESOLVED` | **[S-E: Annexure F, N-E-17, BR-12]** |
| HD-12 | Data fiduciary | **Per entity and per category.** *"Assign per entity and per data category"* **[A-N14]** — **the corpus's own instruction, unexecuted** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the instruction; **`UNKNOWN`** as to the assignment | **[S-A: A-N14]** |
| HD-13 | Data sharing | **The hardest cell in the matrix.** Cross-entity member, volunteer, customer and donor data requires a basis, an agreement, a purpose limitation and a retention rule for each pair — **none exists** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-A: A-N14]** |
| HD-14 | Banking | **N mandates. A-N16 is a BLOCKER for one** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the blocker | **[S-A: A-N16]** |
| HD-15 | Money in | **A central pool.** *"a central pool (donations, grants, founder capital, Krytos)"* **[S-B: §22.2]** — **which is R7's funding architecture stated in one line and `UNRESOLVED`** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** + `UNRESOLVED` | **[S-B: §22.2, §9, E-03]** |
| HD-16 | Money out | **Cross-charging, royalties, management fees and distributions between entities** — all absent from the eight-priority waterfall | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the absence | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-G: GC-01]** |
| HD-17 | Related-party / transfer pricing | **Pervasive.** *"Strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`* — *`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`* | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** |
| HD-18 | Employment | **Per entity, with mobility arrangements that do not exist** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-19 | Third-party contracts | **Per entity.** Rule E-58's *"the legal entity is named accurately"* becomes a **discipline across N entities** — **and the rule exists precisely because the corpus anticipated it** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-58]** |
| HD-20 | Insurance | **Group programme possible; cross-entity liability is a broker question** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — *`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`* | **[S-G: Part XVI]** |
| HD-21 | Donations & donor disclosure | **A donor must be told which entity receives the gift and what the group does with it.** *"No concealment on request"* **[S-E: Rule E-59]** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** as to the rule | **[S-E: Rule E-59]** |
| HD-22 | Public representation | **Rule E-58's two-tier naming exists for exactly this case**: parent in legal and funding contexts, operating name in service contexts | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-E: E-11, Rule E-58]** |
| HD-23 | Members & volunteers | **A member of one entity is not a member of another.** The 10M-member figure **[S-ST: §5]** would be spread across entities with no instrument governing movement | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S-G: UE-08…UE-11]** |
| HD-24 | Dissolution | **Per entity, with group unwinding.** Art.19 covers one entity only | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to Art.19's scope | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |
| HD-25 | Succession | **Per entity plus the group holder.** The corpus's *"in parallel"* **[S-A: A-14 S5]** becomes **N+1 parallel regimes** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the mechanism; **`UNKNOWN`** as to the content | **[S-A: A-14 S5]** |
| HD-26 | Mission lock | **The strongest available lock.** *"A federated structure where the mission is held by a body that no single shareholder controls"* **[S-ST: F-11 (iv)]** — **and the corpus notes it *"dilutes single-owner control … reduces founder flexibility — a deliberate trade"* [§5 Scenario D]** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** | **[S-ST: F-11 item 7, §5 Scenario D]** |
| HD-27 | Pilot authorisation | **A pilot of one entity does not authorise a group.** **[S-A: A-L7]** classifies federation as **LATER** | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the timing — *`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`* | **[S-A: A-L7]** |
| HD-28 | Audit & filings | **N audits plus any consolidation** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | — |
| HD-29 | Disputes & forum | **Inter-entity disputes have no mechanism.** A *"standards body"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** is proposed and undefined | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`**; **`UNKNOWN`** as to powers | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** |
| HD-30 | Scale ceiling | **The highest of the eight.** **10M is contemplated only here** — *"No legal form comfortably holds a 10M-member public institution under one private owner"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario E]** is the negative statement of R7's necessity | **`SOURCE-SUPPORTED`** as to the statement | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario E]** |

**[A] R7 summary:** **R7 is the only model that reaches the architecture's own stated scale, and the corpus says so twice — once as a proposal ([S-ST: §5 Scenario D]) and once as a negation ([S-ST: §5 Scenario E]).** **It is classified `LATER` by [S-A: A-L7] and is therefore not available for the pilot. Its content — four sibling projects, a standards body, a common constitution — is `OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS`.**

**[P] Counting rule.** Cells carrying a **dual** status (for example *"`SOURCE-SUPPORTED` as to the rule and `UNKNOWN` as to its application"*) are counted **once**, under the **first** mandated label in the cell. **Sub-markers** — `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`, `REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`, `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`, `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`, `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`, `QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL` — are recorded **inside** a mandated status and are counted separately in §V.10; **they do not change the 240-cell distribution above.**

## V.11 The four findings Part V produces

**[A] Finding 1 — the three dimensions on which everything depends are the three this instrument may not judge.** **HD-01 (legal personality), HD-03 (entity formation) and HD-27 (pilot authorisation) carry `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION` in all eight models — 24 cells, exactly one in ten of the matrix.** No model in Part IV can be declared workable or unworkable here. **That is a structural property of the question, not a limit of the analysis: every other dimension in the matrix is downstream of these three.**

**[A] Finding 2 — R8, the present state, is the most unknown model by a factor of more than four.** **R8 carries 18 `UNKNOWN` cells; R1 and R4 carry 4 each; R3 and R6 carry 7.** The fourteen cells that separate R8 from R1 are not fourteen professional opinions. **They are consequences of one undisclosed fact about Krytos and one undecided fact about the relationship.**

**[A] Finding 3 — authority, not structure, is the second-largest blockage, and it is the same blockage v1.3-G and v1.3-F already recorded.** **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` and `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL` together mark 22 cells**, concentrated in **HD-06, HD-07 and HD-08** — governance above the Council, CEO appointment and removal, and reserved matters. **These are the same three items v1.3-G carried as `UE-01`, `UE-25` and `LDR-C26`, and v1.3-F carried as `C-D-17`.** **Part XI determines whether Krytos adds a governance layer or merely relocates the same unresolved authority one level up. Its answer: it relocates it.**

**[A] Finding 4 — the mandatory markers cluster where ownership meets money, and GC-01 survives every model.** **10 of the 13 `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE` cells sit at HD-05, HD-12, HD-16, HD-17 and HD-18** — tax status, data fiduciary role, money out, related-party dealings and employment. **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE` appears in all 16 tax and transfer-pricing cells across all eight models, including R1 and R4, where there is no group at all** — because the founder is on both sides of every transaction. **GC-01 is therefore not a Krytos problem, and no choice among R1…R8 closes it.**

# PART VI — THE FOUR-PILLAR TEST APPLIED TO THE KRYPTOS RELATIONSHIP

## VI.0 The four pillars and the test's permitted vocabulary

**[S: Ch.3]** names four pillars: **Social Service · Community Network · Protection / First Response · AI Business Network.** **[S-G: EP-04]** records the standing instruction: *"The structure must be examined against **all four**, not the social-service component."*

**[P] Rule H-16 (No Viability Verdict).** **This Part renders no yes/no judgement on whether any model supports any pillar.** Every cell that would otherwise carry such a judgement carries **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`**, with the **specific question a professional must answer** stated beside it. **A pillar is not "satisfied" or "unsatisfied" by a relationship model; it is *conditioned* by it, and the condition is a professional question.**

**[P] Cell vocabulary:** **`COMPATIBLE IN PRINCIPLE — VALIDATION REQUIRED`** (the model does not obviously defeat the pillar, and the defeating question is nameable) · **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** (the corpus itself records a conflict) · **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** (no judgement possible) · **`NOT CONDITIONED BY THE RELATIONSHIP`** (the pillar's requirement is the same under every model) · **`UNKNOWN`**.

## VI.1 Pillar 1 — SOCIAL SERVICE

**[S: Ch.3 pillar 1; Ch.15]** — education, food, health-support, cleaning, plantation, clothing, donations, community infrastructure, emergency, animal and environment support.

| Model | Effect on the social-service pillar | Cell status | The specific question a professional must answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **Donation receipt, tax exemption and charitable purpose sit wholly inside one entity.** No upstream claim on donations. **[S-B: §9]**'s funding problem is unchanged but no longer has a "Krytos" candidate | **`COMPATIBLE IN PRINCIPLE — VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | *Does the chosen form permit charitable objects, donation receipt and any exemption claim, and does it permit the other three pillars in the same objects clause?* **[S-ST: Annexure B]**: *"objects clauses permitting all four pillars"* |
| **R2** | **The source's own risk statement applies: *"May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos"* [S: App. A §3.2 row 1].** A donor's gift may enter a body whose other activities are undescribed | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *Can charitable funds be held inside a structure whose parent conducts other activities, and what ring-fencing is required?* **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** |
| **R3** | **Donations reach an entity that is separately controlled but commonly owned.** Disclosure of the common control is required by **[S-E: Rule E-59]** | **`COMPATIBLE IN PRINCIPLE — VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | *Does common control affect eligibility for any exemption, CSR receipt or donor-deductible status?* **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **R4** | **The founder's other interests are a disclosed conflict, not an ownership chain.** Closest to a conventional charitable founder model | **`COMPATIBLE IN PRINCIPLE — VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | *What conflict-of-interest and related-party discipline does the form require where the founder is on both sides?* |
| **R5** | **Service delivery may be performed by a third body.** Members receiving services are receiving them from an entity they did not choose | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *May a charitable service be delivered under a services contract, and what duty does the recipient-owning entity owe?* |
| **R6** | **The service may depend on licensed IP the entity does not own.** *"A licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** applies to service continuity | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *What continuity obligation does a service-providing institution owe its beneficiaries if its operating system is licensed and withdrawable?* |
| **R7** | **Services may be delivered across multiple entities.** **The corpus's own scale case: 10M members is contemplated only here [S-ST: §5 Scenario D/E]** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Which entity holds the charitable purpose, which receives the donation, and which owes the beneficiary?* |
| **R8** | **Cannot be assessed.** The pillar's most basic requirement — a person to receive a donation and owe a duty — is undetermined | **`UNKNOWN`** | *Every question above, in order* |

**[A] Pillar 1 finding:** **the social-service pillar is the only pillar whose requirement is identical under R1, R3 and R4 — a legal person with charitable objects.** It differs under R2, R5, R6 and R7 because those models place something between the beneficiary and the entity. **The corpus's single source-recorded tension is at R2, and it is the corpus's own words.**

## VI.2 Pillar 2 — COMMUNITY NETWORK

**[S: Ch.3 pillar 2]** — residents, local relationships, need identification, volunteer coordination, trustworthy local information flow.

| Model | Effect on the community-network pillar | Cell status | The specific question a professional must answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **Volunteers and members contract with one body.** **[S-G: UE-08…UE-11]** remain open but are unaffected by the relationship | **`NOT CONDITIONED BY THE RELATIONSHIP`** | *What is the legal status of a volunteer and of a member, and what duties does the entity owe each?* |
| **R2** | **A network of 10M people would sit inside a body owned by one private owner.** **[S-ST: §11.1]**: *"the institution's identity and its legal continuity are the same object, held by one person."* **[S-ST: §18.2]** warns against misclassification as *"A private company / agency"* | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *Can a mass-membership voluntary network be held inside a privately owned structure without converting its character?* **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** |
| **R3** | **Members join one entity; the controller's other ventures are separate.** No member relationship with Krytos arises | **`NOT CONDITIONED BY THE RELATIONSHIP`** | *Must the common control be disclosed to members and volunteers, and if so how?* |
| **R4** | As R3, with the founder's connection personal rather than corporate | **`NOT CONDITIONED BY THE RELATIONSHIP`** | *What disclosure does a volunteer receive about the founder's other interests?* |
| **R5** | **Volunteer coordination, verification and records may be operated by a third body** — which is a data question (Part IX) and a duty question simultaneously | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Who owes the duty of care to a volunteer whose records are processed by another body?* |
| **R6** | **The verification system, marks and content used to recruit and coordinate may be licensed** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Can a network be built on a brand and system the network's entity does not own?* |
| **R7** | **The network becomes multi-entity.** *"Legally distinct state entities bound by a common constitution, standards body and brand licence"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** — **membership mobility between entities is undescribed** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Is a member of one entity a member of the group? Can they move? Who holds the register?* |
| **R8** | **Cannot be assessed** | **`UNKNOWN`** | — |

**[A] Pillar 2 finding:** **the community-network pillar is the least relationship-sensitive of the four — three models leave it unconditioned — and the most scale-sensitive.** Its only CRITICAL tension is at R2, and it is stated by the stress test in the corpus's own words.

## VI.3 Pillar 3 — PROTECTION / FIRST RESPONSE

**[S: Ch.3 pillar 3; Ch.16]**; **[S-A: A-06…A-11]** — nine-step sequence, six DO-NOTs, risk-tier matrix, *"do not investigate or punish."* **[S-G: EP-10]**: *"The pillar is liability-heavy and insurance-dependent … Cover is written on a legal person."*

| Model | Effect on the protection pillar | Cell status | The specific question a professional must answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **Liability sits wholly in one entity.** Cover is written on that person. **No parent's balance sheet and no parent's exposure** | **`COMPATIBLE IN PRINCIPLE — VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | *What cover is available at all for acts of "protection"/first response by a mass-membership voluntary organisation?* **[S-A: A-10 §9.1 L4]** — **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`** |
| **R2** | **The source's own words: protection risk may be *"mix[ed] … with broader Krytos"* [S: App. A §3.2 row 1].** A first-response incident inside Sewak Sathi could reach an undocumented parent — **or the parent's other activities could reach the protection work** | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *Does the parent's exposure attach to the operating body's protection activities, and can it be separated?* **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** |
| **R3** | **Liability separate; insurance separate.** Cross-liability between commonly controlled bodies is a real question | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Does common control create any cross-liability, and does it affect the availability or price of cover?* **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`** |
| **R4** | **Liability separate.** The founder's personal exposure as an individual acting in a protection incident is a distinct question | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *What personal exposure does a founder have for protection acts performed by an entity he founded but does not own?* **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`** |
| **R5** | **If a third body operates the dispatch, verification or escalation system, its failure is a protection failure** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Who is liable when a shared service fails during a first response?* |
| **R6** | **The protection system itself may be licensed and withdrawable** | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *May a protection function depend on IP the performing entity does not own?* |
| **R7** | **Protection is local; a group structure multiplies the number of liable persons across jurisdictions.** *"State-specific trust/society regimes"* **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *Which entity is liable for a protection act performed in a state where a different group entity operates?* |
| **R8** | **Cannot be assessed — and this is the pillar where non-assessment has the highest human cost** | **`UNKNOWN`** | *Every question above, in order* |

**[A] Pillar 3 finding:** **this is the pillar most sensitive to the relationship and least able to tolerate `UNKNOWN`.** Insurance cover is written on a legal person **[S-G: EP-10]**; **there is presently no legal person to write it on.** **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL` in every cell — never "COVERAGE EXISTS".**

## VI.4 Pillar 4 — AI BUSINESS NETWORK

**[S: Ch.3 pillar 4; Ch.17; App. A §13]**. **[S: App. A §1.3]**: *"The organisation is not converted into a conventional NGO-only model; the business component remains central."* **[S-ST: R4]**: *"the three natures conflict."*

| Model | Effect on the AI-business pillar | Cell status | The specific question a professional must answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **Commercial activity inside a charitable-purpose entity — the sharpest form of the conflict the corpus already records.** *"Restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure"* **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *May the chosen charitable form conduct commercial activity at all, on what terms, and with what application-of-income consequences?* **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **R2** | **The business pillar is the one Krytos was most plausibly created for.** But **"Krytos" is never described as a business** — the four sibling projects are named once and never described | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *What does Krytos actually do, and does its activity interact with Sewak Sathi's commercial pillar?* — **unanswerable without KDP-01…KDP-03** |
| **R3** | **Two commonly controlled bodies, one charitable and one commercial, transacting with each other.** *"A brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *What transfer-pricing and related-party regime applies between a charitable entity and a commonly controlled commercial one?* **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`**; **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** |
| **R4** | **The founder's commercial interests are personal.** The entity's commercial activity is its own | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *What conflict discipline applies where the founder personally benefits from the entity's commercial activity?* |
| **R5** | **Commercial services supplied between the bodies — the purest related-party case in the matrix** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | *At what price, on what terms, documented how?* **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **R6** | **The operating system, the brand and the content are the commercial assets — and their owner is unknown.** *"Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | **`TENSION IDENTIFIED IN SOURCE`** | *Who owns the IP that generates the commercial pillar's revenue, and on what licence?* **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **R7** | **A group in which the commercial and charitable pillars are separate entities — the structure [S-ST: R4 (iii)] proposes: *"a holding structure with the mission held separately from both"*** | **`SOURCE-PROPOSED`** — but **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** as to viability | *Can the mission be held separately from both pillars, by whom, and with what enforceability?* |
| **R8** | **Cannot be assessed.** **GC-01 lives here and is rated `CANNOT BE ASSESSED` [S-D: §50.5]** | **`UNKNOWN`** | *Every question above, in order* |

**[A] Pillar 4 finding:** **the AI-business pillar is the only pillar where the corpus itself proposes a structural answer — separation — and the only pillar whose central conflict (GC-01) is unaffected by every model.** **R1 does not solve GC-01; it relocates it inside one entity.** **R7 does not solve GC-01; it relocates it between entities.** **Part VII.**

## VI.5 The four-pillar test, summarised

| Pillar | Models that leave it unconditioned | Models with a source-recorded tension | Models requiring validation | Cells `UNKNOWN` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Social Service** | **none** | **R2, R6** | R1, R3, R4, R5, R7 | **1** (R8) |
| **2 — Community Network** | **R1, R3, R4** | **R2** | R5, R6, R7 | **1** (R8) |
| **3 — Protection / First Response** | **none** | **R2, R6** | R1, R3, R4, R5, R7 | **1** (R8) |
| **4 — AI Business Network** | **none** | **R1, R3, R6** | R2, R4, R5, R7 | **1** (R8) |
| **Totals (32 cells)** | **3** | **8** | **17** | **4** |

**[A] Three conclusions, and no verdict:**

1. **No model leaves all four pillars unconditioned.** **R1, R3 and R4 leave one each — the community-network pillar; R2, R5, R6, R7 and R8 leave none.** This is not an argument against any model — it is a statement that **the relationship is load-bearing for every pillar, and that only the community network is indifferent to it in any model at all**.

2. **R2 and R6 are the only models with a source-recorded tension in three of the four pillars** — R2 at pillars 1, 2 and 3; R6 at pillars 1, 3 and 4. **R2's are stated in the corpus's own risk column at [S: App. A §3.2 row 1] and at [S-ST: §18.2]; R6's in [S-A: §11.2]'s withdrawal statement.** **The two tensions are of different kinds: R2's arise from *mixing* the pillars in one owner, R6's from *licensing* the pillars' operating assets from one.** **This paper does not conclude that either model is unsuitable. It records that the corpus's own analysis identifies tensions in both that it does not identify in R1, R3 or R4 — and that the corpus never notices this, because it never compares the models.**

3. **R8 — the present state — leaves all four pillars `UNKNOWN`.** **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION` or an equivalent validation status appears in 17 of 32 cells, and a source-recorded tension in 8.** **No professional can perform that validation until KDP-01…KDP-04 are supplied, because the validation question in every one of those 17 cells begins *"under what relationship?"***

---

# PART VII — GC-01: DEEP ANALYSIS AND DECISION TREE

## VII.1 GC-01 as v1.3-G stated it

**[S-G: GC-01]** — **GOVERNANCE/SOURCE CONFLICT 01**, verbatim in substance:

> **The conflict.** The **waterfall's priority 4** (*founder compensation*) and **priority 8** (*residual surplus / founder upside*) are **`APPROVED`** and are **reserved matters** — **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-D: LDR-C16, LDR-C26]**. But **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** records that a nonprofit-type form carries *"**restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**."*
>
> **The corpus's own rating:** **[S-D: §50.5]** rates the conflict **`CANNOT BE ASSESSED`**.

**[S-G]** additionally recorded that GC-01 is one of the **four material gaps** behind its verdict **B**, and that it **cannot be closed without a professional opinion on the entity form** — which itself cannot be given without knowing the Krytos relationship.

## VII.2 Why GC-01 is a Krytos question and not only an entity question

**[A]** **Six dependencies, each traceable to a specific Krytos fact:**

| # | Dependency | Why it is a Krytos fact | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Who pays priority 4?** | *"Founder compensation"* names a **recipient**, not a **payer**. If Sewak Sathi is a Krytos division, the payer is Krytos and its capacity to pay is `UNKNOWN` (H-Q11, D-9). If Sewak Sathi is separate, the payer is the entity and its capacity depends on the waterfall itself | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-B: §9]** |
| **2** | **Who is entitled to priority 8?** | *"Residual surplus / **founder** upside"* names a **founder**. **[S: Ch.4]** places *"Property, brand, strategy"* with *"**Krytos / CEO**."* **H-PD-7**: the corpus does not distinguish the two. If the residual belongs to Krytos, the founder's entitlement runs through Krytos's own instruments — which are `OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS` | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S: Ch.4]**; **[S: App. A §3.5]** |
| **3** | **Does a "separate structure" already exist?** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** says the commercial waterfall may need *"separate structure."* **Krytos may be that structure, may be the thing from which separation is required, or may be neither.** **H-Q13, H-Q14 are `UNKNOWN`** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]**; **[S-ST: R4 (ii), (iii)]** |
| **4** | **Is priority 5's *"organisation service fee"* a Krytos payment?** | The waterfall's **only** inter-body flow. **Its recipient is unnamed.** Under R5 or R6 it is Krytos; under R1 or R4 it cannot be | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **Part V HD-16** |
| **5** | **Are priorities 4 and 8 related-party transactions?** | If Krytos and Sewak Sathi are related, **every** payment between them is. *"Strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**. **Under R1 they are not related — but the founder still is** | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**; Part V HD-17 |
| **6** | **Can a reserved matter be decided at all?** | Priorities 4 and 8 are **reserved matters** — not delegable to any interim authority **[S-A: A-14 §13.3; S-D: LDR-C26]**. **The holder is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` ([S-F: Rule F-10]).** If the holder is Krytos, **H-Q28 applies: the body that would decide is itself undefined** | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-F: Rule F-10, C-D-17]** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-13 (new).** **GC-01 was correctly identified by v1.3-G as an entity-form conflict and incorrectly bounded.** **It is also a Krytos-relationship conflict, and the two are separable: the entity-form limb requires a professional opinion (which requires `LDR-U08`); the Krytos limb requires a disclosure (KDP-01…KDP-04).** **The Krytos limb can be closed first, and closing it narrows the professional question rather than answering it.**

## VII.3 The GC-01 decision tree — nine nodes

**[P]** **The tree does not resolve GC-01.** It states, for each branch: **the question · who answers it · what validation is required · what changes downstream if it is answered · what remains blocked either way.**

```
                         GC-01
                            │
        ┌───────────────────┴───────────────────┐
        │                                       │
    N-1: Is Krytos a legal person?          N-2: Is Sewak Sathi
        │                                       a legal person?
   ┌────┴────┐                                  │
   │         │                             ┌────┴────┐
 YES        NO                             YES       NO
   │         │                              │         │
   │      N-3: Then "founder upside"        │      N-4: Then priorities 4
   │         runs to whom?                  │         and 8 have no payer
   │         AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED           │         and no recipient
   │                                        │         UNKNOWN
   │                                        │
   └──────────────► N-5: Are Krytos and Sewak Sathi related persons?
                            │
                    ┌───────┴───────┐
                   YES              NO
                    │                │
              N-6: Transfer-     N-7: Then is the *founder*
              pricing regime     a related party in his
              applies —          personal capacity?
              REQUIRES               │
              QUALIFIED TAX     ┌────┴────┐
              ADVICE           YES        NO
                                │          │
                          N-8: Conflict   N-9: GC-01's
                          discipline —    Krytos limb closes;
                          holder          entity-form limb
                          AUTHORITY       remains open —
                          UNRESOLVED      REQUIRES
                                          PROFESSIONAL
                                          STRUCTURE
                                          VALIDATION
```

## VII.4 The nine nodes, specified

| Node | **The question** | **Who answers** | **Validation required** | **What changes downstream if answered** | **What stays blocked either way** | Terminal status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **N-1** | **Is Krytos a legal person?** | **The founder/CEO — as discloser. This is KDP-01.** No professional is needed to answer it; a professional is needed to interpret the answer | None to answer; **corporate counsel** to interpret | Answers H-Q01, H-Q02, H-Q13, H-Q14, H-Q15. Collapses Model D-1, D-2. Determines whether N-3 exists at all | **GC-01's entity-form limb.** **`LDR-U08`.** **`SR-01`.** **A-N16** | **`UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`** |
| **N-2** | **Is Sewak Sathi Sanstha a legal person?** | **The founder/CEO, by decision — this is `LDR-U08` / GC-02** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** — corporate and tax counsel on OPT-A…OPT-J | Answers HD-01 for every model; unblocks **A-N16**; makes Rule E-58 executable | **GC-01's priority-8 limb**, because "founder upside" presupposes a surplus and a person entitled to it | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** |
| **N-3** | **If Krytos is not a legal person, to whom does *"residual surplus / founder upside"* run?** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`.** The holder of the reserved matter is undetermined **[S-A: A-14 §13.3; S-D: LDR-C26]**, and **[S-F: Rule F-10]** shows the body that would determine it is partly constituted by it | **Governance counsel** — `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL` | Would determine whether priority 8 is a distribution, a contractual payment, a salary, or nothing | **Everything downstream of priority 8**: reserves policy, donor disclosure, tax treatment | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** |
| **N-4** | **If Sewak Sathi is not a legal person, who pays priority 4 and who receives priority 8?** | **`UNKNOWN`.** In the only model where this arises (R2 with *"Internal Krytos division"*), the payer would be Krytos — **whose banking authority is H-Q11, `UNKNOWN`** | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** + **corporate counsel** | Would determine whether the waterfall is an internal allocation or a set of payments between persons | **A-N16** — a division has no mandate; **GC-02**; the entire pilot | **`UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`** |
| **N-5** | **Are Krytos and Sewak Sathi related persons?** | **Depends on N-1 and on KDP-04 (ownership and control).** If N-1 is NO, the question is whether the founder is the common link | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** | Determines HD-17 for all models; determines whether priority 5's *"organisation service fee"* is an intra-group charge | **GC-01's entity-form limb**; **the amount of any fee** — no numeric figure exists or may be invented | **`UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`** |
| **N-6** | **If related, what transfer-pricing and related-party regime applies to priorities 4, 5 and 8?** | **A qualified tax professional — no corpus actor can answer** | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`**; *"strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**; documentation requirements | Determines whether the waterfall as drafted is executable at all, and what documentation each priority needs | **The waterfall's amounts** — **`UNKNOWN`**, and no figure may be invented (v1.3-G Rule G-2) | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **N-7** | **If not related corporately, is the *founder* a related party in his personal capacity?** | **A qualified tax professional, on facts supplied by the founder/CEO** | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** | Determines whether priorities 4 and 8 remain related-party transactions under R1 and R4 — **i.e. whether GC-01 survives independence** | **GC-01's entity-form limb** | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **N-8** | **If the founder is a related party, who holds the conflict-of-interest discipline over priorities 4 and 8?** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`.** *[S-A: A-14 §13.3]* makes both reserved matters; **[S-F: C-D-17]** finds no determined holder; **[S-F: Rule F-10]**: *"the body that would decide them is itself partly constituted by them"* | **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** | Would determine whether an independent body must exist before any payment under priorities 4 or 8 can be made | **Every payment under priorities 4 and 8** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** |
| **N-9** | **Does closing the Krytos limb close GC-01?** | **No.** This node is the tree's own answer about itself | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | Establishes that **KDP-01…KDP-04 narrow GC-01 without resolving it**: after them, GC-01 reduces to a single professional question about one entity form | **GC-01 as a whole**, until **`LDR-U08`** is decided and counsel opines | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** |

## VII.5 The tree's terminal distribution

| Terminal status | Nodes | Count |
|---|---|---|
| **`UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`** | N-1, N-4, N-5 | **3** |
| **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** | N-3, N-8 | **2** |
| **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** | N-6, N-7 | **2** |
| **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** | N-2, N-9 | **2** |
| **Resolved by this instrument** | **none** | **0** |
| **Total nodes** | N-1 … N-9 | **9** |

**[A] Eight of nine nodes terminate in a status that this instrument may not cross.** **The ninth — N-1 — is a disclosure.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-14 (new): GC-01's tree has exactly one non-professional, non-authority node, and it is the root.** **N-1 requires no counsel, no CA, no CS, no broker and no governance body. It requires one person to state whether Krytos exists in law.** **Three of the four inputs v1.3-G said would move its verdict from B to A are of this kind (H-Q01…H-Q04 / KDP-01…KDP-04). GC-01 cannot be closed by them — but it cannot even be *framed* for counsel without them.**

## VII.6 What this Part does not do

**[P]** **GC-01 is not resolved. It is not narrowed to a recommendation. No option is preferred. No amount is stated. No conclusion is drawn about whether founder compensation or founder upside is permissible, appropriate, excessive or lawful.** **Those are decisions for authorised human decision-makers on professional advice, and the corpus assigns them to a holder that does not exist.**

**[P]** **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** on every tax limb · **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** on every authority limb · **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** on every structure limb · **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** on the separation question at **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]**. **No permission to operate informally is granted or implied anywhere in this Part.**

# PART VIII — KRYPTOS IP INVENTORY

## VIII.0 Method and vocabulary

**[P]** **Seventeen asset classes.** For each, the inventory records **four positions in strict order**:

1. **`SOURCE OWNER`** — the owner the corpus **explicitly states**, with its ID. If none, the cell reads **`NONE STATED`**.
2. **`PROPOSED OWNER`** — the owner a **proposal** would produce, with its ID and its `PROPOSED` status. If none, **`NONE PROPOSED`**.
3. **`UNKNOWN OWNER`** — the owner that **cannot be determined** from the corpus, and the reason.
4. **`REQUIRED DECISION`** — the decision that must be taken before the class can be used, held, licensed, valued, insured or defended.

**[P] Rule H-17 (No IP Is Assigned By This Instrument).** **Nothing in Part VIII transfers, vests, licenses or creates any right.** `copyright` has **zero occurrences** in the corpus (v1.3-G finding); `trademark` and `trade mark` appear only as **questions**; no assignment, no licence, no registration and no schedule exists. **`A-N13` is `UNRESOLVED`.**

**[P] The governing sentence, repeated because every row depends on it:** **[S-A: §11.2]** — *"**Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution; a licensor could withdraw it.**"* **The corpus states the consequence of an ownership it never identifies.**

## VIII.1 The inventory — H-IP-01 … H-IP-17

| ID | Asset class | `SOURCE OWNER` | `PROPOSED OWNER` | `UNKNOWN OWNER` | `REQUIRED DECISION` | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## VIII.2 The inventory's result

| Position | Count | Items |
|---|---|---|
| **`SOURCE OWNER` stated** | **0 of 17** | — |
| **A controller, custodian or function stated without ownership** | **6** | H-IP-02 *(controller)* · H-IP-06 *(function)* · H-IP-14, H-IP-15, H-IP-16, H-IP-17 *(custody)* |
| **`PROPOSED OWNER`** | **4** | H-IP-01, H-IP-02, H-IP-03, H-IP-04 — all four via **[S: App. A §3.5]** *"brand ownership"* or **[S-E: N-E-17]**, all four **`PROPOSED`** and two of them **`UNRESOLVED`** in the same corpus |
| **Owner wholly `UNKNOWN`** | **17 of 17** | every class |
| **Blocked by an existing corpus item** | **7** | H-IP-01, H-IP-03, H-IP-04, H-IP-05 (`A-N12` / Annexure F) · H-IP-06 (`A-N13`) · H-IP-09 (`A-N14`) · H-IP-16 (`A-N16`) |

**[A] Finding H-PD-15 (new).** **Not one of the seventeen asset classes has a stated owner.** **The corpus records a *controller* for one (the parent name), a *function* for one (the operating system, assigned to a National department), and *custody* for five — and custody is not ownership.** **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]**'s dual-custody requirement is the corpus's most developed IP provision and it protects assets whose owner nobody has identified.

**[A] Finding H-PD-16 (new): the corpus's own two statements about the operating system point in opposite directions, and no instrument notices.** **[S: App. A §4.2]** assigns *"Build and govern the internal operating system"* to **National Technology — inside Sewak Sathi**. **[S-A: §11.2]** states *"Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution"* — **outside it**. **Both are `SOURCE` statements. They cannot both be true of the same asset unless the department builds something it does not own, which no instrument says.** **This is recorded, not resolved.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-17 (new): the parent name is more settled than the operating name, and this is backwards.** **[S-E: Annexure F]** gives the parent name **"Krytos"** a controller and the status **"APPROVED as parent context"**, while the operating name, mark, palette, domain and IP are all **`UNRESOLVED`** with availability unvalidated. **An institution whose public name is unvalidated has nonetheless approved the name of a body whose legal existence is unknown.** **Part XXI NS-12.**

**[P] Rule H-18 (No Implied Assignment).** Nothing in this Part may be read as assigning any asset to Krytos, to the founder, to Sewak Sathi or to any other person. **Every cell ends in a decision that has not been taken.**

# PART IX — DATA RELATIONSHIP MAP

## IX.0 Method and vocabulary

**[P]** **Eleven data classes × seven relationship attributes = 77 cells.** The map covers the flows **Krytos ↔ Sewak Sathi ↔ members / volunteers / customers / donors / the public**.

**[P] The corpus's governing provisions:**
- **`A-N14`** — data fiduciary **`UNRESOLVED`**; the required action is *"Assign **per entity and per data category**."* **[S-A]**
- **[S-A: §11.2]** — *"the legal data fiduciary is **presumably** the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**."* **Hedged by its author; the only statement of its kind in the corpus**
- **[S: Ch.5]** — an **AI & Data** department exists as a National function
- **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** — dual custody of platform, cloud, repository, domain, payment gateway, channels, social accounts and statutory portals

**[P] Cell vocabulary:** **`SOURCE`** = the corpus states it · **`PROPOSED`** = the corpus proposes it · **`UNKNOWN`** = the corpus records nothing · **`PRESUMED`** = the corpus states it with an express hedge · **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** = a data-protection professional must determine it.

**[P] Rule H-19 (No Data Role Is Assigned).** **No cell in Part IX makes Sewak Sathi or Krytos a controller, a fiduciary, a processor or a joint controller.** **The corpus has not done so, and this instrument may not.**

## IX.1 The eleven data classes

| ID | Data class | Population | Sensitivity indicated by the corpus |
|---|---|---|---|
| **H-DC-01** | **Member data** | Verified members — the 10M figure at **[S-ST: §5]** is contemplated only at R7 | **[S-A: §11.2]** treats the member estate as the institution's core data asset |
| **H-DC-02** | **Volunteer data** | Volunteers and local coordinators | **[S-A: A-06…A-11]** — volunteers act in protection incidents |
| **H-DC-03** | **Customer / commercial-client data** | AI-business-network counterparties | **[S: Ch.17; App. A §13]** |
| **H-DC-04** | **Donor data** | Donors, CSR counterparties, grant-makers | **[S-E: §12]** requires accurate disclosure **to** donors |
| **H-DC-05** | **Public / community data** | Non-personal community need data, local information flow | **[S: Ch.3 pillar 2]** — *"trustworthy local information flow"* |
| **H-DC-06** | **Beneficiary / service-recipient data** | Persons receiving social service | **[S: Ch.15]** |
| **H-DC-07** | **Protection and first-response incident data** | Persons in risk situations | **The most sensitive class in the corpus.** **[S: Ch.16]** six DO-NOTs; *"do not investigate or punish"*; **[S-ST: §29]** — *"a documented, neutral, **non-authority-bearing interface**"* |
| **H-DC-08** | **Worker / employee / contractor data** | Paid staff and engaged contractors | **[S-G: Part VIII]** classification unresolved |
| **H-DC-09** | **Financial and transaction data** | Payments, fees, donations, the waterfall | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **A-N16** |
| **H-DC-10** | **Platform telemetry, logs and AI training data** | System-generated | **[S: Ch.5]** AI & Data; **H-IP-08** |
| **H-DC-11** | **Governance and decision records** | Minutes, approvals, escalations, reserved-matter decisions | **[S-D: Part 34]**; **[S-A: A-14]** |
