| **Deletion** | 0 | 0 | 0 | **11** | **11** |
| **Breach responsibility** | 0 | 0 | 0 | **11** | **11** |
| **Totals** | **5** | **0** | **2** | **70** | **77** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-18 (new).** **Seventy of seventy-seven cells are `UNKNOWN`. The seven that are not unknown are five `SOURCE` statements — four of dual custody and one general record-keeping principle, none of which is a data role — and two presumptions, one of which is the corpus's own hedge, *"presumably … ultimately Krytos."*** **No retention period, no deletion route and no breach responsibility exists for any data class, under any relationship model, anywhere in 3.87 MB.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-19 (new): the Krytos presumption makes the data position worse, not better.** If *"ultimately Krytos"* is correct, then **every one of the eleven classes has a controller whose identity, jurisdiction, governing documents and succession are unknown (H-Q01…H-Q04, H-Q12)** — and **the four sibling projects named at [S: App. A §3.1] become potential recipients of member, beneficiary and protection data with no sharing basis recorded.** **If it is incorrect, `A-N14` is simply unexecuted.** **Both branches require `A-N14`'s own instruction: *"Assign per entity and per data category"* — which cannot be performed until the entities exist and are identified.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-20 (new): H-DC-07 is the cell that makes Part IX non-deferrable.** **Protection and first-response data concerns persons in risk situations. The corpus requires Sewak Sathi to be a *"documented, neutral, non-authority-bearing interface"* [S-ST: §29] and forbids it to *"claim authority it does not have."*** **It presently cannot say who bears data authority over that class — not for itself, and not for any parent.** **This cell is `UNKNOWN` in seven of seven attributes.**

**[P] Rule H-20 (No Lawful Basis Is Asserted).** **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** applies to all 77 cells as a class. **No lawful basis, no retention period, no deletion right, no breach-notification duty and no cross-border transfer position is asserted, implied or assumed anywhere in Part IX.**

# PART X — MONEY FLOWS

## X.0 Method and vocabulary

**[P]** **Eight flow directions × eight attributes = 64 cells.** The attributes are exactly those the brief requires: **`PERMITTED`** · **`PROHIBITED`** · **`UNKNOWN`** · **`REQUIRES AGREEMENT`** · **`REQUIRES TAX VALIDATION`** · **`RELATED PARTY`** · **`TRANSFER PRICING`** · **`GOVERNANCE APPROVAL`**.

**[P] Rule H-21 (No Amount, No Rate, No Threshold).** **No figure appears in Part X.** The corpus contains no budget, no fee schedule, no royalty rate, no capital amount and no pilot threshold, and **none may be invented** (v1.3-G Rule G-2, adopted). **[S-B: E-03]** — *"Who funds the centre before priority-5 income exists?"* — is **`UNRESOLVED — no source instrument`**, and it stays that way.

**[P] Rule H-22 (`PERMITTED` Requires a Source).** A cell reads **`PERMITTED`** only where the corpus contains an explicit permission. **Absence of a prohibition is `UNKNOWN`, not `PERMITTED`.**

**[P] The governing corpus provisions:**
- **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** — the eight-priority waterfall, of which **priority 4** is *founder compensation*, **priority 5** an *"organisation service fee"* and **priority 8** *"residual surplus / founder upside"*; priorities 4 and 8 are **reserved matters**
- **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** — *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure"*
- **[S-B: §9, E-03, B-03, §22.2, EV-4]** — *"founder capital / **Krytos** / validated donation / accrued-and-waived fee"*; *"a central pool (donations, grants, founder capital, **Krytos**)"*; **EV-4** asks for the *"Authority to advance funds"*
- **[S-B: §8.6]** — *"**implies** the founder bears those costs — consistent with Krytos ownership"* — **the corpus's own hedge**
- **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** — *"a brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"*
- **A-N16** — the **bank-mandate BLOCKER**, preserved

## X.1 The eight flows

| ID | Direction | What the corpus says it is for |
|---|---|---|
| **H-MF-01** | **Krytos → Sewak Sathi** | Pre-revenue funding of the centre; seed reserves |
| **H-MF-02** | **Sewak Sathi → Krytos** | Organisation service fee (priority 5); residual surplus (priority 8); any royalty |
| **H-MF-03** | **Founder → Sewak Sathi** | Founder capital; personal guarantee of early costs |
| **H-MF-04** | **Sewak Sathi → Founder** | Founder compensation (priority 4); founder upside (priority 8) |
| **H-MF-05** | **Donor / grant-maker → Sewak Sathi** | Donations, CSR, grants — the *"validated donation"* candidate |
| **H-MF-06** | **Customer / commercial client → Sewak Sathi** | AI-business-network revenue |
| **H-MF-07** | **Sewak Sathi → workers, vendors, contractors** | Operating costs, priorities 1–3 |
| **H-MF-08** | **Sewak Sathi ↔ State / City / Mohalla units** | The *"central pool"* and its disbursement **[S-B: §22.2]** |

## X.4 The four findings Part X produces

**[A] Finding 1 — not one of the eight flows is executable today, and only one is permitted by an explicit source.** **Every flow requires an agreement that does not exist and tax validation that has not been sought.** **A-N16 blocks the mandate; GC-02 blocks the person.** **This is not a Krytos finding — it is a v1.3-G finding restated at the level of individual flows, and it holds under all eight relationship models.**

**[A] Finding 2 — the founder is a related party in every model, including the two with no Krytos.** **H-MF-03 and H-MF-04 are `RELATED PARTY` and `TRANSFER PRICING` under R1 and R4 as much as under R2.** **GC-01 therefore does not close if the Krytos relationship is dissolved; it changes character from an intra-group question to a founder-conflict question.** **Part VII N-7.**

**[A] Finding 3 — the two flows that would connect Krytos and Sewak Sathi are the two least determined in the matrix.** **H-MF-01's authority is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` and its source is `UNRESOLVED — no source instrument`.** **H-MF-02's recipient is unnamed and its entitlement is a reserved matter with no holder.** **The corpus names Krytos as a funding candidate four times and never once establishes its capacity, its authority or its entitlement.**

**[A] Finding 4 — the corpus's own hedge is the honest summary of Part X.** **[S-B: §8.6]**: *"**implies** the founder bears those costs — **consistent with** Krytos ownership."* **Two hedges in one sentence: "implies" and "consistent with."** **Neither is a determination, and no instrument converts them into one.** **Part XXI NS-08.**

**[P] Rule H-23 (No Flow Is Authorised).** **Nothing in Part X authorises any payment, advance, distribution, fee, salary, donation receipt or transfer.** **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** applies to all eight flows. **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to H-MF-01, H-MF-02 and H-MF-04. **No permission to operate informally is granted or implied.**

# PART XI — GOVERNANCE DEPENDENCY

## XI.1 The question this Part answers

**[P]** Two questions, in order:

1. **Does the Krytos relationship preserve, worsen or resolve the corpus's existing governance conflict — CEO vs National Council?**
2. **Does Krytos add a governance layer?**

**[P] Rule H-24 (The Existing Conflict Is Preserved Verbatim).** **`CEO vs National Council — AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` is carried forward unchanged.** **This Part does not resolve it, does not prefer either side, and does not treat Krytos's existence as an answer to it.**

## XI.2 The existing conflict, as the corpus states it

| Element | Source | Position |
|---|---|---|
| **The Council's authority is unresolved** | **[S: App. A §1.1]** | *"National Council authority is unresolved **because ownership remains with CEO/Krytos**."* **The corpus's own causal explanation names Krytos** |
| **The Council does not own the organisation over the CEO** | **[S: Const. Art.3]**; **[S: Ch.4]**; **[S: App. A §3.4]** | *"unless a future legal charter explicitly changes that."* **No charter exists** |
| **The CEO has final executive authority** | **[S: App. A §3.5]** | *"`PROPOSED`"* — **not approved** |
| **The Council is proposed as an oversight body** | **[S: App. A §1.2]** | *"an oversight/standards/advisory body with delegated powers, while CEO retains final executive authority subject to law and charter"* — approval required: *"Yes — governance charter."* **The charter does not exist** |
| **The CEO cannot appoint the CEO** | **[S-F: C-D-17]** | *"the CEO cannot appoint the CEO, and nobody else is determined"* |
| **The body that would decide is partly constituted by the decision** | **[S-F: Rule F-10]** | *"the body that would decide them is itself partly constituted by them"* |
| **v1.3-G's carrying of the item** | **[S-G: UE-01, UE-25]** | `UE-01` — legal entity structure; `UE-25` — governance authority |

**[A] Finding H-PD-21 (new): the corpus attributes its own central governance conflict to Krytos, and never examines the attribution.** **[S: App. A §1.1]** gives the reason the Council's authority is unresolved as *"because ownership remains with CEO/Krytos."* **That is a causal claim about a body with no governance content.** **If Krytos has no governing authority, no officers, no meetings and no instrument — and the corpus records none — then the stated cause of `LDR-U01` is not Krytos but the absence of any constituted authority at all.** **The conflict is preserved; its stated cause is not.**

## XI.3 Four incompatible hierarchies

**[A]** **H-PD-3 restated with its governance consequence.** The corpus draws the top of the organisation **five different ways**:

| # | Source | The hierarchy as drawn | Is Krytos a governance layer? | Is the Council present? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **[S: App. A §1.1]** | Council at the top; **Krytos does not appear** | **No** | **Yes — at the top** |
| **2** | **[S: App. A §3.3 Diagram 3B]** | `KRYTOS (Owner / product ecosystem)` → `Founder / CEO` → Council → National → State → City → Mohalla → Members | **Yes — above the CEO** | **Yes — third** |
| **3** | **[S: Ch.1]** and **[S: Ch.4]** | `KRYTOS → SEWAK SATHI SANSTHA → NATIONAL → STATE → CITY → MOHALLA → VERIFIED MEMBERS`; and in Ch.4 a single box **`KRYTOS / Founder-CEO`** | **Yes — but merged with a person** | **No — absent from both** |
| **4** | **[S-B: §3.2]** | Founder/CEO → National → State → City → Mohalla | **No** | **No** |
| **5** | **[S-ST: §5 item 1]** | **Seven levels**, Krytos counted as one, with latency at each | **Yes — and counted as a cost** | **Yes** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-22 (new): the National Council appears at the top in one hierarchy, third in another, and is absent from three.** **No instrument reconciles them. `LDR-U01` is therefore not merely unresolved as between CEO and Council — **the Council's own position in the structure is drawn five ways.*** **Governance counsel is required: `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`.**

## XI.4 Does Krytos add a governance layer? — the attribute test

**[P]** A governance layer is identifiable by what it does. **Twelve attributes** are tested. **For each, the corpus's provision for the National Council and for the CEO is set against its provision for Krytos.**

| # | Governance attribute | **National Council** | **Founder / CEO** | **Krytos** | Krytos a layer? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GA-01** | **Defined responsibilities** | **Yes** — **[S: App. E]**, 20 rows; **[S-D: Part 34]**, 30 rows | **Yes** — **[S: App. A §3.5]**; **[S: Ch.4]** | **`NONE`** | **No** |
| **GA-02** | **Defined outputs** | **Yes** — **[S: App. A §4.2]**, ten National domains | **Yes** — **[S: App. A §3.5]** | **`NONE`** | **No** |
| **GA-03** | **Decision rights enumerated** | **Yes** — may / may-not lists **[S: App. A §3.5]** | **Yes** — may / may-not lists | **`NONE`** — only *"Ownership … strategy"* as an authority **type** **[S: Ch.4]** | **No** |
| **GA-04** | **A row in the authority map** | **Yes** — **[S-D: Part 34]** | **Yes** | **`NONE` — no Krytos row in 30** | **No** |
| **GA-05** | **A column or row in the approval matrix** | **Yes** — **[S: App. E]** | **Yes** | **`NONE` — no Krytos row in 20** | **No** |
| **GA-06** | **A RACI line** | **Yes** — **[S-D: §34.2]**, 15 activities | **Yes** | **`NONE`** | **No** |
| **GA-07** | **An escalation route** | **Yes** — crisis escalation to the CEO **[S: App. A §3.5]** | **Yes** — top of the escalation chain | **`NONE`** — no route runs to Krytos anywhere | **No** |
| **GA-08** | **Meeting cadence / quorum** | **`PROPOSED`** in v1.3-B/E governance material | **Not applicable** | **`NONE`** | **No** |
| **GA-09** | **Officers / composition** | **Yes** — composition addressed **[S-D: LV-8]** *"board composition"* | **Yes** — one named person, **Vasu** | **`NONE`** — no officer, director, member or shareholder of Krytos is named anywhere | **No** |
| **GA-10** | **Removal mechanism** | **`PROPOSED`** | **`UNKNOWN`** — **[S-F: C-D-17]** | **`NONE`** | **No** |
| **GA-11** | **Reserved matters held** | **No** — *[S: App. A §3.5]* lists what the Council **may not** approve alone | **Partly** — **[S-F: SR-01]** assigns *"Legal entity structure under Krytos"* to the Founder/CEO | **`NONE` stated** — **but reserved matters must be held by somebody, and no body is determined (`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`)** | **No — but the gap points at it** |
| **GA-12** | **Succession provision** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **Partly** — **[S-A: A-14 S5]** addresses founder death at entity level | **`NONE`** — **named as existing (*"Krytos's own succession"*) and described as *"outside this architecture entirely"* [S-A: §11.2]** | **No — but it operates anyway** |

## XI.5 The answer

**[A] Krytos adds a governance layer in position and in nothing else.**

1. **In position: yes.** **Diagram 3B places it above the CEO; [S: Ch.1] places it above the institution; [S-ST: §5] counts it as a seventh level with latency.** **Three of the five hierarchies put Krytos at the top.**

2. **In content: no.** **Zero of twelve governance attributes are provided.** Krytos has no responsibilities, no outputs, no decision rights, no row in either authority instrument, no RACI line, no escalation route, no cadence, no officers, no removal mechanism, no reserved matters and no succession provision.

3. **In effect: it displaces the question rather than answering it.** **[S: App. A §1.1]** attributes the Council's unresolved authority to Krytos's ownership. **Since Krytos has no governance content, the attribution transfers an unresolved question to an undefined body.** **`LDR-U01` is not resolved by Krytos; it is relocated above the Council, to a place with no rules.**

4. **And it creates a new, unrecorded dependency.** **GA-11 and GA-12 are the two attributes where the absence is load-bearing.** Reserved matters must be held by somebody **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; succession must operate somehow **[S-A: A-14 S5]**. **In both, the corpus's only candidate is a body with no governance content — and in both, the corpus's own status is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` or *"outside this architecture entirely."***

**[A] Finding H-PD-23 (new): Krytos is the only body in the architecture that is above the CEO and below nothing, and that has no rules.** **Every other level — National, State, City, Mohalla, Members — has responsibilities, outputs, may/may-not lists and escalation routes. **The top of the organisation is the only undocumented level.***

**[P] Rule H-25 (No Governance Authority Is Conferred).** **Nothing in Part XI confers authority on Krytos, on the founder/CEO, on the National Council or on any other body.** **`CEO vs National Council — AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` is preserved verbatim.** **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** applies to GA-08, GA-10, GA-11 and GA-12 and to the reconciliation of the five hierarchies. **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to any instrument that would create, limit or transfer governance authority.

# PART XII — SUCCESSION STRESS

## XII.0 Method

**[P]** **Eleven succession scenarios × nine dimensions = 99 cells.** The test asks, for each scenario: **what actually happens, on the present record?**

**[P] Rule H-26 (No Succession Mechanism Is Invented).** **No will, no nomination, no transfer instrument, no power of attorney, no buy-sell arrangement, no vesting schedule, no continuity plan and no interim authority is posited.** Where the corpus provides none, the cell reads **`UNKNOWN`**. **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** where the corpus shows the deciding body is itself undetermined.

**[P] The corpus's two governing statements:**
- **[S-A: §11.2]** — *"**The entity's control follows Krytos's own succession, which is outside this architecture entirely.**"*
- **[S-A: A-14 S5]** — on founder death: *"the entity's own succession law and **Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]**"*; and the residual-risk note: they *"operate in parallel and **outside this architecture**."*

**The nine dimensions:** **SX-D1** who holds authority immediately · **SX-D2** which instrument governs · **SX-D3** what happens to the entity · **SX-D4** what happens to the brand and IP · **SX-D5** what happens to bank mandates · **SX-D6** what happens to members, volunteers and beneficiaries · **SX-D7** what happens to contracts and workers · **SX-D8** what happens to the mission · **SX-D9** the recovery route.

## XII.1 The eleven scenarios

| ID | Scenario | Is it contemplated by the corpus? |
|---|---|---|
| **H-SX-01** | **Founder death** | **Yes** — **[S-A: A-14 S5]**; **[S-ST: F-11]**; **[S-ST: §11.1]** |
| **H-SX-02** | **Founder temporary incapacity** | **Partly** — **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** dual custody anticipates access continuity; no authority continuity |
| **H-SX-03** | **Founder permanent incapacity** | **No** — not distinguished from death anywhere |
| **H-SX-04** | **Founder sells Krytos** | **Yes** — **[S-ST: F-11 item 1]**: *"if that person … **sells Krytos**"* |
| **H-SX-05** | **Krytos is dissolved or becomes insolvent** | **No** — never contemplated |
| **H-SX-06** | **Founder resigns as CEO but retains ownership** | **No** — the corpus does not separate the two roles |
| **H-SX-07** | **Founder is removed** | **No** — no removal mechanism exists for the founder in any instrument |
| **H-SX-08** | **Control of Krytos passes to a third party by any means** | **Partly** — *"a **future owner of Krytos**"* **[S-ST: §1]**; *"an **unrelated owner of the parent**"* **[S-A: A-13 §12.2]** |
| **H-SX-09** | **Sewak Sathi is dissolved** | **Yes** — **[S: Const. Art.19]**; nothing about the parent |
| **H-SX-10** | **Governance deadlock between CEO and Council** | **Yes** — this is **`LDR-U01`** in operation; **[S-F: C-D-17]** |
| **H-SX-11** | **Founder personal bankruptcy or a creditor claim** | **No** — never contemplated |

| **SX-D2** instrument | 0 | **7** | 0 | 0 | 0 | **4** *(H-SX-01 two in parallel; H-SX-04 and H-SX-08 Krytos's own, `OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS`; H-SX-09 Art.19)* | 0 | **11** |

| **SX-D9** recovery route | **6** *(one carrying `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`)* | **5** *(4 `NONE RECORDED`; 1 `NONE`)* | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **11** |
| **Column totals** | **59** | **12** | **4** | **2** | **2** | **14** | **6** | **99** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-24 (new): across 99 cells, the corpus supplies a determinate answer in 14 — and only three of the fourteen are a mechanism.** **All three are the same mechanism, **Art.19**, at H-SX-09 (SX-D2, SX-D3 and SX-D5).** **The other eleven determinate cells say only that something is *unchanged*, *ends*, is *paralysed*, *transfers*, is *closed*, or that the corpus's own instrument is named but lies outside it — and a further 12 cells read `NONE` or `NONE RECORDED`.** **There is no recovery route in any of the eleven scenarios, and in five of them the corpus records that none exists.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-25 (new): the "two regimes in parallel" statement is the corpus's most consequential succession finding and its least examined.** **[S-A: A-14 S5]** says that on founder death the entity's succession law **and Krytos's governing documents** operate in parallel. **For that to be true, Krytos must have governing documents, must have a succession mechanism, and must have a relationship to the entity through which that mechanism travels.** **All three are `UNKNOWN` (H-Q04, H-Q12, H-Q05).** **The corpus asserts a parallel regime and supplies neither of its two elements.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-26 (new): three scenarios are never contemplated and two of them are worse than the one that is.** **H-SX-03 (permanent incapacity), H-SX-05 (Krytos dissolution) and H-SX-11 (founder bankruptcy) appear nowhere.** **H-SX-03 is worse than death because no succession law is triggered and no interim authority exists. H-SX-05 is worse than a sale because there is no buyer to negotiate with.** **The corpus's entire succession analysis is written around one event — the founder's death — and one mechanism: the sale of Krytos.**

**[P] Rule H-27 (No Continuity Is Assumed).** **Nothing in Part XII may be read as providing continuity of authority, service, funding, contract or mission in any scenario.** **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** applies to SX-D1 and SX-D9 in every scenario. **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to any instrument that would create a succession mechanism.

# PART XIII — INDEPENDENCE TEST

## XIII.0 Method

**[P]** **Seven conditions of independence.** For each, the test asks: **on the present record, is Sewak Sathi Sanstha independent of Krytos in this respect?**

**[P] Permitted conclusions — the five the brief requires:** **`STRUCTURALLY INDEPENDENT`** · **`CONTRACTUALLY DEPENDENT`** · **`OPERATIONALLY DEPENDENT`** · **`ECONOMICALLY DEPENDENT`** · **`UNKNOWN`**.

**[P] Rule H-28 (No Condition Is Declared Satisfied).** **`STRUCTURALLY INDEPENDENT` is recorded only where the corpus establishes independence positively.** **Where the corpus is merely silent, the conclusion is `UNKNOWN` — because silence about a dependency is not evidence of independence.**

## XIII.1 The seven conditions

| ID | Condition | What independence would require | Conclusion | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H-IN-1** | **Infrastructure independence** — the platform, code, cloud, domain, repositories, channels and statutory portals | Sewak Sathi holds or controls them in its own right, with no third party able to withdraw them | **`UNKNOWN`**, with a **`OPERATIONALLY DEPENDENT`** risk recorded | **For independence:** *[S: App. A §4.2]* assigns *"Build and govern the internal operating system"* to **National Technology** — inside the institution. **Against:** *[S-A: §11.2]* — *"Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution; **a licensor could withdraw it**."* **H-PD-16: the two statements conflict and nothing reconciles them. H-IP-06 is `UNRESOLVED`** |
| **H-IN-2** | **Financial independence** — funding, reserves, banking, the ability to pay for operations before revenue exists | Sewak Sathi has its own funding source, its own account and its own mandate | **`ECONOMICALLY DEPENDENT`** — **as proposed**, with the dependency itself `UNKNOWN` as to source | **[S-B: §9, E-03]**: *"Who funds the centre before priority-5 income exists? — **UNRESOLVED — no source instrument**"*, with candidates *"founder capital / **Krytos** / validated donation / accrued-and-waived fee."* **[S-B: B-03]** seeds reserves from *"founder capital, **Krytos**, or a validated donation"* `[V]`. **[S-B: §8.6]**: *"**implies** the founder bears those costs — **consistent with** Krytos ownership."* **A-N16 blocks the mandate** |
| **H-IN-3** | **IP independence** — ownership or an irrevocable licence to everything the institution operates | Sewak Sathi owns, or holds an irrevocable licence to, the name, marks, content, code and data | **`UNKNOWN`** — **all 17 asset classes have no stated owner (Part VIII)** | **`A-N12`, `A-N13` `UNRESOLVED`**; **[S-E: N-E-17]** four alternatives; **[S-E: BR-12]**; **[S-F: XXIV.2]** *"grantor is undetermined"*; **[S-A: §11.2]** withdrawal risk |
| **H-IN-4** | **Brand independence** — the right to be called what it is called, without another person's permission | Sewak Sathi holds its own name and marks, validated as available | **`UNKNOWN`**, with a **`CONTRACTUALLY DEPENDENT`** alternative recorded | **The operating name, mark, palette and domain are `UNRESOLVED` and availability is unvalidated** **[S-E: Annexure F]**; **the parent name is *"APPROVED as parent context"*** — **H-PD-17**; **[S-E: N-E-17]** offers *"Founder/Krytos-owned"* and *"licensed"* as live alternatives, **each of which is a dependency** |
| **H-IN-5** | **Data independence** — control of member, volunteer, beneficiary, donor and protection data in its own right | Sewak Sathi is the controller/fiduciary of every class, with no upstream claim | **`UNKNOWN`** — **70 of 77 cells in Part IX are `UNKNOWN`** | **`A-N14` `UNRESOLVED`**; *"Assign per entity and per data category"* unexecuted; **[S-A: §11.2]** *"**presumably** the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**"* — **the corpus's own hedge points upstream** |
| **H-IN-6** | **Governance independence** — the ability to make and execute decisions without another body's concurrence | A constituted authority with defined rights, and no undocumented body above it | **`UNKNOWN`** — **and the corpus attributes the existing conflict to Krytos** | **[S: App. A §1.1]**: *"National Council authority is unresolved **because ownership remains with CEO/Krytos**"*; **`LDR-U01`**; **[S-F: C-D-17]**; **[S-F: Rule F-10]**; **Part XI GA-01…GA-12: Krytos provides none of the twelve** |
| **H-IN-7** | **Contractual and personnel independence** — the ability to employ, engage, contract and be sued in its own name | A legal person with capacity, and no second employer or counterparty | **`UNKNOWN`** — **`STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT` on GC-02 / HD-01** | **No legal person is established** (**[S-F: P0-A]** *"the entity exists"* is unmet); **[S-G: Part VIII]** classification unresolved; **Part V HD-18** is `UNKNOWN` under R2 and `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` under every other model |

## XIII.2 The test's result

| Conclusion | Conditions | Count |
|---|---|---|
| **`STRUCTURALLY INDEPENDENT`** | **none** | **0 of 7** |
| **`CONTRACTUALLY DEPENDENT`** | H-IN-4 *(as one of four recorded alternatives)* | **1, alternative only** |
| **`OPERATIONALLY DEPENDENT`** | H-IN-1 *(as a recorded risk)* | **1, risk only** |
| **`ECONOMICALLY DEPENDENT`** | H-IN-2 | **1** |
| **`UNKNOWN`** | H-IN-1, H-IN-3, H-IN-4, H-IN-5, H-IN-6, H-IN-7 | **6 of 7** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-27 (new).** **Not one of the seven conditions of independence is established, and six of the seven are `UNKNOWN`.** **The single condition with a positive conclusion — H-IN-2, economic dependence — is a conclusion about a *proposal*, not about an arrangement: Krytos is a **candidate** funder in an `UNRESOLVED` decision with `no source instrument`.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-28 (new): independence is not the same as the absence of a dependency, and the corpus conflates them.** **[S: App. A §3.1]** — *"not … an independent body that **automatically supersedes the founder/CEO**"* — is the corpus's only sentence containing the word *"independent"* in connection with Sewak Sathi's status. **It denies automatic supersession of a person. It does not assert dependence on a body, and it does not exclude independence in law.** **H-Q21 and NS-13.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-29 (new): the seven conditions are not equally hard.** **H-IN-2, H-IN-3, H-IN-4 and H-IN-5 require decisions and instruments. H-IN-1 and H-IN-7 require a legal person. H-IN-6 requires a governance charter.** **None requires an answer about Krytos — but all seven are currently unanswerable *because* the Krytos relationship is undefined, since each asks "independent of what?"**

**[P] Rule H-29 (No Independence Is Declared).** **This Part does not find Sewak Sathi independent, and does not find it dependent.** **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** applies to H-IN-1, H-IN-3, H-IN-4 and H-IN-5 if any dependency is confirmed. **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to every instrument that would create or remove a dependency.

---

# PART XIV — CONTROL TEST

## XIV.0 Method

**[P]** **Sixteen domains of control.** For each: **who controls it, on the present record?**

**[P] Permitted conclusions — the six the brief requires:** **`EXPLICIT`** (the corpus expressly assigns control) · **`PROPOSED`** (a proposal assigns it) · **`IMPLIED`** (control follows necessarily from something the corpus states) · **`UNKNOWN`** · **`PROHIBITED`** (the corpus forbids it) · **`REQUIRES DECISION`**.

**[P] Rule H-30 (No Control Is Assigned To Krytos By Default).** **A domain is assigned to Krytos only where the corpus says so.** *"Under Krytos"* is **not** an assignment of control; it is a phrase whose object is undefined. **Where the corpus says *"Krytos / CEO"* or *"CEO/Krytos"*, the cell records **both** names and the fact that the corpus does not distinguish them (H-PD-7).**

| **H-CT-16** | **Public representation and disclosure** | **`EXPLICIT`** as to duty, **`BLOCKED`** as to content | **Rule E-58**: the legal entity named accurately, Krytos absent from service artefacts; **Rule E-59**: *"Any person asking who owns or controls the organisation receives an accurate answer in plain language … An ownership structure that can only survive not being asked about is not a governance structure."* **The accurate answer today is a conditional sentence** | **[S-E: Rule E-58, E-59]**; **[S-E: §12]** |

| **`PROPOSED`** | H-CT-02, H-CT-04 | **2** |

| **`PROHIBITED`** | H-CT-01 to the National Team and to State Teams; **and no domain is prohibited to Krytos anywhere in the corpus** | **1** |
| **`REQUIRES DECISION`** | H-CT-01, H-CT-02, H-CT-03 *(amendment)*, H-CT-08, H-CT-09 | **5** |
| **`BLOCKED` by an existing corpus item** | H-CT-07, H-CT-10, H-CT-13, H-CT-16 | **4** |

## XIV.3 The four findings Part XIV produces

**[A] Finding 1 — Krytos controls nothing explicitly, and nothing prohibits it from controlling anything.** **Of sixteen domains, Krytos is named in two (H-CT-02, in the compound *"Krytos / CEO"*; and H-CT-12, in the hedge *"ultimately Krytos"*).** **It is assigned no domain outright.** **Equally, no instrument places any limit on Krytos's control — because no instrument describes it.**

**[A] Finding 2 — `IMPLIED` is empty, and that is the point of Rule H-30.** **The natural inference from *"the organisation is owned by Krytos"* and *"legal structure under Krytos"* is that Krytos controls domains H-CT-01 through H-CT-16.** **The corpus never draws that inference in an operative provision; it draws it only in a hedge (*"presumably"*) and in a risk statement (*"whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system"*).** **Recording it as `IMPLIED` would legalise by wording, which the brief forbids.**

**[A] Finding 3 — the five `REQUIRES DECISION` domains are the five that determine everything else.** **H-CT-01 (form), H-CT-02 (brand), H-CT-03 (mission amendment), H-CT-08 (founder compensation) and H-CT-09 (residual surplus).** **Three of the five are reserved matters with `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` holders; two are GC-01.** **All five are downstream of KDP-01…KDP-04.**

**[A] Finding 4 — four domains are `BLOCKED`, and none of them is blocked by Krytos.** **H-CT-07, H-CT-10, H-CT-13 and H-CT-16 are blocked by the absence of a legal person and by A-N16.** **The Krytos relationship does not create these blockers; it prevents them from being resolved, because a professional cannot advise on a mandate, a contract or a disclosure for an entity whose relationship to its stated owner is undefined.**

**[P] Rule H-31 (No Control Is Conferred Or Denied).** **Part XIV records the corpus's position. It confers no control on Krytos, on the founder/CEO, on the National Council or on any other body, and it denies none.** **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** applies to H-CT-05, H-CT-08, H-CT-09 and to the reconciliation of *"Krytos / CEO"* with *"Founder/CEO."* **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to every instrument that would confer or limit control.

# PART XV — THE MINIMUM KRYPTOS DECISION PACK: KDP-01 … KDP-12

## XV.0 What this Part is

**[P]** **Twelve items.** **Ten are disclosures** — facts or documents that exist in the world and are not in the corpus. **Two are decisions** — choices that no disclosure can make. **Nothing beyond these twelve is included, because nothing beyond them is required by a source.**

**[P] Rule H-32 (The Pack Is Minimum, Not Sufficient).** **KDP-01…KDP-12 make Krytos definable. They do not make Sewak Sathi registrable, bankable, insurable, employable or pilotable.** Those require **`LDR-U08`**, **A-N16**, **GC-02** and the professional validations at Part XVI. **The pack's purpose is to make those questions answerable, not to answer them.**

**[P] Rule H-33 (No Item May Be Answered By Inference).** **Each item requires a document or a statement of fact from its holder.** **Where the corpus contains a proposal that could be mistaken for an answer, the item records the proposal and marks it `PROPOSED — NOT AN ANSWER`.**

## XV.1 The twelve items

| ID | Item | Type | **What must be established** | Evidence required | Holder of the information | What it unblocks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **KDP-01** | **Krytos's legal status** | **Disclosure** | Whether Krytos **is** a legal person; and if so, its exact legal name and form. If it is not, what it is: a brand, a project label, a trading style of a natural person, or nothing | **Certificate of incorporation / registration, or a deed, or a statement that none exists.** No document in the corpus | **Founder/CEO — H-Q27** | H-Q01, H-Q02 · Model D-1, D-2 · Part II Model C-1, C-2 · N-1 · HD-01 across all models · **UE-24 limb 1** |
| **KDP-02** | **Krytos's jurisdiction** | **Disclosure** | The jurisdiction of formation or registration; registered office; the law governing its constitution | **Registration document; registered-office record** | **Founder/CEO** | H-Q04 · D-3 · HD-04, HD-05 · **UE-24 limb 2** · every cross-border and state-heterogeneity question at **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** |
| **KDP-03** | **Krytos's governing documents** | **Disclosure** | The constitution, articles, deed, agreement or charter — **and the v1.0 document, `KRYTOS ORGANISATION MASTER ARCHITECTURE, CONSTITUTION & LAUNCH SPECIFICATION v1.0`, named four times and never supplied** | **The documents themselves.** **[S-A: A-14 S5]** and **A-V9** both call for them; **`OUTSIDE CURRENT CORPUS`** | **Founder/CEO** | H-Q12 · D-4, D-14 · Part XII SX-D2 in every scenario · **UE-24 limb 3** · **H-PD-1's ambiguity** |

None exist** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** — the body that would decide Sewak Sathi's subordination is itself constituted by that decision **[S-F: Rule F-10]** | Part XIII in full · H-IN-1…H-IN-7 · Rule E-59's accurate answer · **H-PD-27** |

## XV.2 The pack's properties

| Property | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Items requiring disclosure** | **10** — KDP-01…KDP-10 |
| **Items requiring a decision** | **2** — KDP-11, KDP-12 |
| **Items whose holder is one person** | **10 of 12** — the founder/CEO, as discloser |
| **Items whose holder is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** | **2** — KDP-11, KDP-12 |
| **Items requiring a professional to answer** | **0** — **every disclosure is a fact about the world. Professionals are required to *interpret* KDP-01…KDP-10, not to supply them** |
| **Items requiring an architectural change** | **0** |
| **Items requiring expenditure** | **0 recorded — and none may be estimated (v1.3-G Rule G-2)** |
| **Items that exist as documents somewhere** | **At least 4** — KDP-01, KDP-02, KDP-03, KDP-04, **if Krytos exists in law. If it does not, KDP-01's evidence is a statement, not a document** |
| **Items that close `UE-24`** | **4** — KDP-01…KDP-04 are the four limbs v1.3-G named: *"legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership."* **Part XVIII** |
| **Items that close GC-01** | **0** — **Part VII N-9: KDP-01…KDP-04 narrow GC-01 without resolving it** |
| **Items that close `LDR-U08`** | **0** — **KDP-01…KDP-04 make `LDR-U08` *answerable by counsel*; they do not answer it** |
| **Items that close A-N16** | **0** — **A-N16 is preserved as a BLOCKER throughout this instrument** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-30 (new).** **The minimum Krytos decision pack contains ten disclosures held by one person, none of which requires a professional, a budget, a vote or an architectural change.** **The two items that do require a decision — KDP-11 and KDP-12 — cannot be taken until the ten disclosures are made, because both ask a question whose subject is undefined.** **This asymmetry is the whole of Part XXIII's argument for verdict D rather than C.**

**[P] Rule H-34 (The Pack Does Not Select A Relationship).** **Supplying KDP-01…KDP-12 does not choose among R1…R8.** It makes the choice possible. **The choice remains: OPTION SET → CONSEQUENCE ANALYSIS → PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION → HUMAN DECISION.**

# PART XVI — PROFESSIONAL QUESTION PACKS

## XVI.0 Method

**[P]** **Eight packs, forty-eight questions — H-PQ-C01…C06 (corporate), T01…T06 (tax/CA), G01…G06 (governance/CS), L01…L06 (labour), B01…B06 (banking), I01…I06 (IP/trade mark), D01…D06 (data/privacy), N01…N06 (insurance).**

**[P]** Each question carries: **the exact question · the source ID that makes it necessary · why it matters · the evidence required · the output required · the downstream decision it enables.**

**[P] Rule H-35 (Questions, Not Instructions).** **No question asks a professional to confirm a conclusion this instrument has reached, because it has reached none.** **Every question is open.**

**[P] Rule H-36 (Sequencing).** **All forty-eight questions are conditional on KDP-01…KDP-04.** **A professional asked *"what is the effect of the Krytos relationship on X?"* cannot answer until the relationship is described.** **Each pack therefore opens with its own threshold question.**

## XVI.1 Three validation opinions — H-PV-1 … H-PV-3

**[P]** Named here because the front matter and the executive answer refer to them as the inputs that would move the verdict from **B** to **A**.

| ID | Opinion | Who gives it | On what | Prerequisite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H-PV-1** | **RELATIONSHIP OPINION** | **Corporate counsel** | Which of R1…R8 the disclosed facts constitute; what instruments are required to make it so; whether **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED`** | **KDP-01…KDP-04, KDP-10** |
| **H-PV-2** | **TAX OPINION** | **A qualified tax professional / CA** | The tax consequences of the disclosed relationship for H-MF-01…H-MF-08; related-party and transfer-pricing position; the tax limb of GC-01 | **KDP-01, KDP-02, KDP-04, KDP-09** and **`LDR-U08`** |
| **H-PV-3** | **GOVERNANCE OPINION** | **Governance counsel / CS** | Whether Krytos constitutes a governance layer; how `LDR-U01` is to be resolved; who holds the reserved matters; how the five hierarchies are reconciled | **KDP-03, KDP-04, KDP-11** |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H-PQ-C01** | *Is "Krytos" a legal person, and if so what is its exact legal name, form and jurisdiction?* | KP-01…KP-101; H-Q01…H-Q04 | **The threshold question.** Every other question in all eight packs is conditional on it | **KDP-01, KDP-02** | A statement of legal status, or of the absence of one | **N-1**; selection among R1…R8 |
| **H-PQ-C02** | *On the facts disclosed, which of the eight relationship models at Part IV describes the present position — and which describes the intended position?* | Part IV; **[S: App. A §3.2]** | The corpus offers four structure options and never relates them to the Krytos question | **KDP-01…KDP-04, KDP-10** | **H-PV-1** | **KDP-11**; `LDR-U08` |
| **H-PQ-C03** | *Which of the four options at [S: App. A §3.2] remain available if Krytos is not a legal person?* | **[S: App. A §3.2]**; **[S-G: OPT-A…OPT-J]** | *"Internal Krytos division"* and *"separate company/subsidiary"* both presuppose a Krytos that can have a division or a subsidiary | **KDP-01** | A narrowed option set for v1.3-G Part III | **`LDR-U08`**; GC-02 |

**[A] Finding H-PD-31 (new).** **Not one of the forty-eight professional questions is answerable today.** **This is not a criticism of the professionals; it is a property of the questions.** **Every question in every pack asks what follows from a relationship that has not been described.** **KDP-01…KDP-04 are therefore not one input among many — they are the precondition of the entire professional workstream, and they cost nothing but disclosure.**

# PART XVII — DECISION AUTHORITY

## XVII.0 Method

**[P]** **For each KDP item: DECIDER · VALIDATOR · IMPLEMENTER · AUDITOR.** Where the corpus does not determine a role, the cell reads **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`**.

**[P] Rule H-37 (No Automatic Assignment).** **The CEO, the National Council, the founder and Krytos are NOT assigned by default.** A role is filled only where the corpus fills it, with its ID. **This rule exists because the corpus itself assigns *"Legal entity structure under Krytos"* to the Founder/CEO ([S-F: SR-01]) — and that assignment cannot extend to facts about Krytos that the founder may or may not control.**

**[P] Rule H-38 (Disclosure And Decision Are Different Acts).** **For KDP-01…KDP-10 the founder/CEO appears as DISCLOSER, not as DECIDER.** **A disclosure is a statement of fact; it requires no authority beyond knowledge. A decision requires authority — and for KDP-11 and KDP-12 the authority is unresolved.**

## XVII.1 The authority table

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **KDP-01** | **Founder/CEO — as DISCLOSER, not as decider.** No decision is required; a fact is stated | **Corporate counsel** — H-PQ-C01 | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** — implementation would mean recording the fact in the architecture, and **[S-D: preservation table]** records Art.3 as *"Not amended. Not questioned"* | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** — no audit function exists; **[S-D: Part 34]** has no audit row for ownership facts | **[S-F: SR-01]**; **[S-G: UE-24]**: *"the only person who holds this information"* |

## XVII.2 The authority result

| Role | Determined | `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` |
|---|---|---|
| **DECIDER / DISCLOSER** | **10** — KDP-01…KDP-10, as **DISCLOSER** (the founder/CEO) | **2** — KDP-11, KDP-12 |
| **VALIDATOR** | **12** — every item has at least one named professional | **0** |
| **IMPLEMENTER** | **0** | **12** |
| **AUDITOR** | **0** | **12** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-32 (new).** **The Krytos decision pack has ten disclosers, twelve validators, no implementers and no auditors.** **Not one of the twelve items has a body that would carry the answer into the architecture, and not one has a body that would check that it did.** **This is the same defect v1.3-F found at institutional level (**[S-F: Rule F-10]**) and v1.3-G found at structural level (**`UE-25`**); it appears here in its purest form, because the items concern a body that has no governance content at all (Part XI GA-01…GA-12).**

**[A] Finding H-PD-33 (new): validation without implementation is the corpus's characteristic failure mode.** **Every one of the twelve items can be validated by a professional and none can be implemented by an authority.** **The consequence is predictable and is already visible in the corpus: `A-N12`, `A-N13`, `A-N14` and `A-N16` each state a required action — *"Determine"*, *"Assign"*, *"Assign per entity and per data category"* — **and none names an actor.** **Four of the corpus's sixteen carried-forward items are of this shape.**

**[P] Rule H-39 (No Authority Is Conferred).** **Part XVII confers no authority on the founder/CEO, on Krytos, on the National Council, on any professional or on any future body.** **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** applies to all twelve IMPLEMENTER cells and all twelve AUDITOR cells. **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to any instrument that would create an implementing or auditing authority.

# PART XVIII — CLOSURE OF v1.3-G'S FOUR MATERIAL GAPS

## XVIII.0 The four gaps as v1.3-G stated them

**[S-G: Part XXIV]** returned verdict **B — DECISION-READY WITH MATERIAL GAPS**, and named four gaps standing between **B** and **A**:

| Gap | As v1.3-G described it |
|---|---|
| **GC-01** | The conflict between the **`APPROVED`** waterfall priorities 4 and 8 and **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]**'s *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"*; rated **`CANNOT BE ASSESSED`** at **[S-D: §50.5]** |
| **UE-24** | *"Krytos's own legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership"* — *"Not stated anywhere"* |
| **Availability of OPT-D … OPT-G** | Whether the commercial and hybrid options in **[S-G: Part III]** remain available once the four-pillar and objects-clause tests are applied |
| **LDR-U17** | The **Zero-Capital Local Venture Ecosystem** blueprint, of which **[S: App. A §1.1]** says *"The full separate blueprint file was not attached in this task"* |

**[P] Rule H-40 (Description Is Not Resolution).** **A gap is not closed because it has been described, decomposed, mapped or briefed.** **`CLOSED` requires that the corpus's missing content now exists. `PARTIALLY CLOSED` requires that a limb of the gap is closed and the remainder is precisely specified. Otherwise the gap is `STILL OPEN`.**

## XVIII.1 The closure assessment

| Gap | **Status** | What this instrument did | What remains |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GC-01** | **`STILL OPEN`** — **narrowed, not closed** | **Part VII** decomposed GC-01 into **six Krytos dependencies** and a **nine-node decision tree**; established that the conflict has **two separable limbs** — an entity-form limb requiring counsel and a Krytos limb requiring disclosure (**H-PD-13**); established that the tree's only non-professional node is its root (**H-PD-14**); established that **GC-01 survives every one of the eight relationship models**, including R1 and R4, because the founder is a related party in all eight (**Part X Finding 2**, **N-7**) | **Everything.** No limb is resolved. **Eight of nine nodes terminate in a status this instrument may not cross.** N-9 states expressly that KDP-01…KDP-04 **narrow** GC-01 without resolving it. **[S-D: §50.5]**'s rating of `CANNOT BE ASSESSED` stands |
| **UE-24** | **`PARTIALLY CLOSED`** | **Part III** decomposed UE-24 into **28 questions**; established that **1 is answered** (H-Q27 — the founder is *Vasu*), **1 is proposed**, **25 are unknown**, **1 is authority-unresolved**; established that UE-24 was **correctly identified and materially understated** — v1.3-G framed it as five facts about Krytos, and it is twenty-eight questions, twenty-three of which are about the **relationship** (**H-PD-11**, Rule H-11); **Part XV** reduced it to **ten disclosures held by one person** | **The substance is entirely open.** **KDP-01…KDP-04 are not supplied.** No legal form, no jurisdiction, no governing document, no ownership fact and no objects clause now exists that did not exist before. **`UE-24` remains `UNRESOLVED` in every register that carries it.** **Per Rule H-40, describing the gap is not closing it** |
| **Availability of OPT-D … OPT-G** | **`STILL OPEN`** — **materially narrowed** | **Part V HD-01, HD-03, HD-27** established that **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION` applies to all eight models** at exactly the three dimensions on which option availability turns; **H-PQ-C03** frames the precise corporate question — *which of the four options at [S: App. A §3.2] remain available if Krytos is not a legal person?*; **Part VI** established that **R2 is the only model with a source-recorded tension in three of four pillars**, which bears directly on the commercial and hybrid options | **Availability cannot be determined without KDP-01…KDP-04 plus H-PV-1 and H-PV-2.** Two of OPT-D…OPT-G presuppose a parent that can have a subsidiary; **if KDP-01 returns "not a legal person", those options do not become available — they become void.** This instrument identifies that consequence and does not resolve it |
| **LDR-U17** | **`STILL OPEN`** — **untouched** | **Part I KP-40 … KP-44** established that **four upstream documents are named by the corpus**: two citation forms of the Krytos master architecture (**KP-40, KP-41** — neither supplied, and **H-PD-1** records that they may or may not be one document), the **Zero-Capital Local Venture Ecosystem** blueprint (**KP-43**), of which the corpus says *"The full separate blueprint file was not attached in this task"*, and the 81-page v1.1 specification (**KP-44**), whose **text is preserved** in v1.2 Appendix A although the file is not supplied; **H-PD-1** records the ambiguity between the two citation forms of the Krytos master architecture rather than resolving it | **The document is still not in the corpus.** Nothing in this instrument supplies it. **LDR-U17 remains `UNRESOLVED` exactly as v1.3-F and v1.3-G recorded it** |

## XVIII.2 The result

| Status | Count | Gaps |
|---|---|---|
| **`CLOSED`** | **0** | — |
| **`PARTIALLY CLOSED`** | **1** | UE-24 — **specification closed, substance open** |
| **`STILL OPEN`** | **3** | GC-01 · availability of OPT-D…OPT-G · LDR-U17 |

**[A] Finding H-PD-34 (new).** **This instrument closes none of v1.3-G's four gaps.** **It moves one from *undescribed* to *precisely specified*, and it establishes for all four that **the same ten disclosures stand between the corpus and every one of them.** **That is a real advance and it is not a closure.** **v1.3-G's verdict of B is not improved by this Part; it is explained.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-35 (new): the four gaps are not four gaps.** **GC-01, UE-24, OPT-D…OPT-G availability and — to a lesser degree — LDR-U17 are **one gap with four appearances**: an undefined object at the top of the architecture.** **v1.3-F found the same shape one level down and called `LDR-U08` *"the root"* **[S-F: IV.4]**. `UE-24` is the root above the root.** **Closing KDP-01…KDP-04 does not close any of the four; it makes all four answerable for the first time.**

**[P] Rule H-41 (v1.3-G Is Not Amended Here).** **v1.3-G stands as delivered.** **Part XIX records the amendments this instrument shows to be required; it does not make them.**

# PART XX — PILOT CONSEQUENCE

## XX.0 Method

**[P]** **Fourteen pilot domains.** For each: **does the undefined Krytos relationship block the pilot?**

**[P] Permitted conclusions:** **`BLOCKS PILOT`** · **`CONDITIONAL`** · **`DOES NOT BLOCK`** · **`UNKNOWN`**.

**[P] Rule H-44 (A Blocker Is Not Created Here).** **Where v1.3-F or v1.3-G already recorded a blocker, this Part preserves it and states whether the Krytos relationship adds to it.** **No new blocker is invented, and no existing blocker is lifted.**

**[P] The governing corpus gates:**
- **[S-F: P0-A]** — *"the entity exists"*
- **[S-F: BLK-A-01]** — decide the legal structure; if deferred, *"**The pilot cannot lawfully pay a worker, sign a contract, hold a bank account or be insured**"*
- **[S-F: SR-01]** — *"Legal entity structure under Krytos"* is a **SOURCE-ROOT** item
- **A-N16** — the **bank-mandate BLOCKER**, preserved

## XX.1 The fourteen domains

| ID | **Pilot domain** | **Conclusion** | **Why — on the present record** | Does the Krytos relationship **add** to the blockage? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H-PL-01** | **Entity formation** | **`BLOCKS PILOT`** | **[S-F: P0-A]** requires that *"the entity exists."* **GC-02 is unresolved.** One of its two branches — *"Internal Krytos division"* **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** — is **a Krytos branch whose availability depends on KDP-01** | **Yes — materially.** A branch of GC-02 cannot be evaluated at all until Krytos's legal status is known |
| **H-PL-02** | **Banking** | **`BLOCKS PILOT`** | **A-N16 is a BLOCKER** and is preserved verbatim. **Part XII SX-D5** shows it is compounded in five succession scenarios | **Yes.** **H-Q11**: Krytos's own mandate is undescribed, so a group account cannot be considered either |
| **H-PL-03** | **Worker compensation** | **`BLOCKS PILOT`** | **[S-F: BLK-A-01]**: the pilot *"cannot lawfully pay a worker."* **H-Q18 in Part V is `UNKNOWN` under R2 and `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` under every other model.** **H-PQ-L01**: the employer is unidentified | **Yes.** Under R2 the employer may be Krytos, which is not a legal person on the record |
| **H-PL-04** | **IP** | **`BLOCKS PILOT`** | **Part VIII: 0 of 17 asset classes have a stated owner.** `A-N12`, `A-N13` `UNRESOLVED`. **The name's availability is unvalidated** **[S-E: Annexure F]** | **Yes — decisively.** *"Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution; a licensor could withdraw it"* **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| **H-PL-05** | **Data** | **`BLOCKS PILOT`** | **Part IX: 70 of 77 cells `UNKNOWN`.** `A-N14` `UNRESOLVED`. **No retention period, deletion route or breach responsibility exists for any of eleven classes** | **Yes.** The corpus's own position is *"presumably … ultimately Krytos"* — an upstream controller whose identity is unknown |
| **H-PL-06** | **Contracts** | **`BLOCKS PILOT`** | **[S-F: BLK-A-01]**: the pilot cannot *"sign a contract."* **Rule E-58 requires the legal entity to be named accurately in contractual contexts — and there is no legal entity to name (C-15)** | **Yes.** Rule E-58 is **unexecutable** until KDP-01 and GC-02 are resolved |
| **H-PL-07** | **Insurance** | **`CONDITIONAL`** | **[S-F: BLK-A-01]**: the pilot cannot *"be insured."* **[S-G: EP-10]**: *"Cover is written on a legal person."* **Conditional on H-PL-01.** **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL` — never "COVERAGE EXISTS"** | **Yes.** **H-PQ-N03**: whether a parent relationship affects availability, scope or price cannot be put to a broker until the relationship is described |
| **H-PL-08** | **Governance** | **`CONDITIONAL`** | A pilot can be run by a founder acting personally — **which is what the corpus describes throughout.** But **reserved matters have no holder (`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`)** and **Krytos provides none of twelve governance attributes** | **Yes.** Part XI: the Krytos relationship **relocates** `LDR-U01` above the Council rather than resolving it |
| **H-PL-09** | **Public representation** | **`CONDITIONAL`** | **Rule E-59 forbids the workaround**: *"An ownership structure that can only survive not being asked about is not a governance structure."* **The accurate answer today is Art.3's conditional sentence** | **Yes — this is the domain where the Krytos gap is most visible to the public.** Any member, donor or journalist asking *"who owns this?"* receives an answer that names an undefined body |
| **H-PL-10** | **Business revenue** | **`CONDITIONAL`** | Pillar 4 may generate revenue in principle; **whether the receiving person may conduct commercial activity is the entity-form question (GC-01).** **[S-ST: §29]** prohibits brokering *"access to government schemes for a fee"* | **Yes.** Under R2 the commercial pillar may be *"mix[ed] … with broader Krytos"* **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** — **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** |
| **H-PL-11** | **Pilot authorisation** | **`CONDITIONAL`** | **[S-F: SR-01]** makes *"Legal entity structure under Krytos"* a **SOURCE-ROOT** item; **the authorisation question cannot be framed until the object of "under" is defined** | **Yes — definitionally.** This is the domain the whole instrument concerns |
| **H-PL-12** | **Safeguarding capability** | **`DOES NOT BLOCK`** | Safeguarding design, the six DO-NOTs, the nine-step sequence and the risk-tier matrix **[S: Ch.16; S-A: A-06…A-11]** are **entity-independent**: they can be written, trained and rehearsed without a legal person | **No — but note the qualification.** The capability is entity-independent; **its holder is not.** **H-PQ-L05**: who owes the duty of care is unknown |
| **H-PL-13** | **Social funds** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **No fund exists yet to be affected.** **[S-B: E-03]** is `UNRESOLVED — no source instrument`; **H-MF-05** requires a legal person and any exemption position requires a registration that does not exist | **Unknown.** If Krytos were the source, the characterisation of the advance is **H-PQ-T03** |
| **H-PL-14** | **Reserve custody** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **[S-B: B-03]** proposes seeding reserves from *"founder capital, Krytos, or a validated donation"* `[V]`; **custody of reserves is not addressed anywhere, and no reserve exists** | **Unknown.** Under R2 the reserve may be the parent's asset; under R1 the entity's; **nothing in the corpus distinguishes them** |

| **`CONDITIONAL`** | H-PL-07 insurance · H-PL-08 governance · H-PL-09 public representation · H-PL-10 business revenue · H-PL-11 pilot authorisation | **5** |

| | Count |
|---|---|
| **Domains where the Krytos relationship ADDS to the blockage** | **11** — H-PL-01…H-PL-11 |
| **Domains where it does not** | **1** — H-PL-12 |
| **Domains where the effect is unknown** | **2** — H-PL-13, H-PL-14 |

## XX.3 The three findings Part XX produces

**[A] Finding 1 — the Krytos relationship does not add a new blocker. It makes an existing one unanswerable.** **Every one of the six `BLOCKS PILOT` domains was already blocked by `LDR-U08`, GC-02 or A-N16 in v1.3-F and v1.3-G.** **What the Krytos gap adds is that `SR-01` — *"Legal entity structure **under Krytos**"* — **cannot be put to counsel in a form counsel can answer.** **H-PQ-C01…C06 are all conditional on KDP-01.**

**[A] Finding 2 — one domain does not block, and it is the one with the highest human stakes.** **Safeguarding capability (H-PL-12) can be designed, written, trained and rehearsed today, without a legal person, without a bank account and without an answer about Krytos.** **Its *holder* remains unknown (H-PQ-L05).** **This instrument records that the corpus's most immediately actionable work is also the work whose duty-holder is least determined.**

**[A] Finding 3 — eleven of fourteen domains are worse for the Krytos gap, and none is better.** **No domain in Part XX is unblocked, eased or clarified by the Krytos relationship.** **The relationship functions in the architecture as an answer; **Part XX finds it operating as a question.****

**[P] Rule H-45 (No Pilot Is Authorised).** **Nothing in Part XX authorises a pilot, permits an informal start, or implies that any blocked domain may be proceeded with.** **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** applies to H-PL-01, H-PL-03, H-PL-06 and H-PL-10. **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** applies to H-PL-10, H-PL-13 and H-PL-14. **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`** applies to H-PL-07. **A-N16 is preserved as a BLOCKER.**

# PART XXI — NO-SILENT-CLOSURE AUDIT

## XXI.0 Method

**[P]** **Twenty entries.** Each takes a phrase in the corpus that **reads as though it settles something about Krytos**, and states:

**`SOURCE LANGUAGE`** (verbatim, with its ID) → **`ACTUAL STATUS`** (what the corpus has in fact done) → **`CONSEQUENCE`** (what follows from the difference).

**[P] Rule H-46 (Do Not Legalise By Wording).** **Repeated architectural language is not legal fact.** **A phrase that appears in eight instruments that all inherit one unsupplied source is one occurrence, not eight (Rule H-3).** **No entry in this Part may be read as declaring a source phrase false; each declares only that the phrase does not do the work it appears to do.**

## XXI.1 The audit — NS-01 … NS-20

| ID | **`SOURCE LANGUAGE`** | **`ACTUAL STATUS`** | **`CONSEQUENCE`** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **NS-01** | *"The organisation is **owned by Krytos** under founder/CEO Vasu, **subject to the final legal structure**."* — **[S: Const. Art.3]** | **A preserved decision, expressly conditional.** The condition — *"subject to the final legal structure"* — has not been satisfied in any instrument, and **[S-D: preservation table]** records Art.3 as *"Not amended. Not questioned."* **An article that is not questioned and is conditioned on an unmet condition is not an operative ownership provision** | **`APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` and non-operative at the same time.** Every downstream use of *"owned by Krytos"* inherits the unmet condition. **H-Q01, H-Q02, KDP-01** |

## XXI.2 The audit's result

| Category | Entries | Count |
|---|---|---|
| **Conditional approvals presented as settled** | NS-01, NS-05 | **2** |
| **Descriptions performing the work of definitions** | NS-02, NS-03, NS-04 | **3** |
| **Hedges and inferences presented as positions** | NS-06, NS-08, NS-09, NS-10 | **4** |
| **Rules whose preconditions are unmet** | NS-07, NS-11, NS-12 | **3** |
| **Misreadings the language invites** | NS-13, NS-14 | **2** |
| **Named-but-undescribed objects** | NS-15 | **1** |
| **Assumptions embedded in rationales and analyses** | NS-16, NS-17, NS-18 | **3** |
| **Limits the corpus states honestly** | NS-19, NS-20 | **2** |
| **Total** | NS-01 … NS-20 | **20** |

**[A] Finding H-PD-37 (new).** **Two of the twenty entries — NS-19 and NS-20 — are the corpus being honest about its own limits, and they are the two most useful statements in it.** **[S-A: A-13 §12.2]** and **[§11.2]** tell the reader exactly what the architecture cannot do. **The other eighteen entries are places where the language does more work than the decisions behind it.**

**[A] Finding H-PD-38 (new): none of the twenty entries is a falsehood, and that is the finding.** **Every phrase audited is accurately quoted, correctly attributed and honestly intended.** **The problem is not that the corpus misstates the Krytos relationship. It is that the corpus **describes** a relationship it has never **established**, in language that reads as establishment.** **This is what *"do not legalise by wording"* addresses, and it is why the verdict at Part XXIII is D and not B.**

**[P] Rule H-47 (Nothing Is Silently Closed).** **No entry in Part XXI resolves, corrects, withdraws or amends any source statement.** **Each records the distance between what the language says and what the corpus has decided. A human decision-maker may close any of them; this instrument closes none.**

# PART XXII — EXECUTIVE DECISION PAPER

**[P]** **Twelve sections.** This is the paper a decision-maker reads. **It recommends nothing, selects nothing and resolves nothing.**

## §1 — The question

**What does "under Krytos" mean in the existing architecture, and what minimum facts must be established before Sewak Sathi Sanstha can validly be evaluated as operating under, owned by, controlled by, affiliated with, or independent from Krytos?**

**[S-F: SR-01]** frames the corpus's own version: *"Legal entity structure **under Krytos**."* **[S-D: OQ-05]** asks it directly: *"What is the legal entity structure under Krytos?"* **[S-A: A-V1]** asks counsel for *"Entity form under Krytos."* **All three ask a question whose object is undefined.**

## §2 — What the corpus establishes

**Twenty-four propositions (Part II, Model A).** The material ones:

- Krytos is the top of the master chain **[S: Ch.1]** and is described as *"parent/company context and ownership ecosystem."*
- *"The organisation is owned by Krytos under founder/CEO Vasu, **subject to the final legal structure**"* **[S: Const. Art.3]** — **`APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`, expressly conditional, and recorded as *"Not amended. Not questioned."***
- The National Council does not own the organisation over the CEO *"unless a future legal charter explicitly changes that"* — **and no charter exists.**
- *"Legal structure under Krytos"* is **`UNRESOLVED`**, requires professional validation, and is assigned to the Founder/CEO **[S: Ch.33; S-D: LDR-U08; S-F: SR-01]**.
- **The corpus's constitution cannot bind Krytos's owner:** *"not an unrelated owner of the parent unless instrumented"* **[S-A: A-13 §12.2]**.
- **Krytos's succession governs the entity's control and lies *"outside this architecture entirely"* [S-A: §11.2]**.

## §3 — What the corpus proposes

**Fifteen proposals (Part II, Model B).** None is adopted. **[S-F: Rule F-9]**: *"a proposal that has not been approved has no operative force."* The material ones: four structure options **[S: App. A §3.2]**; *"brand ownership"* as a Founder/CEO function **[§3.5]**; a governance charter **[§1.2]**; brand tier T2 PARENT and Rules E-58/E-59 **[S-E]**; four ascending mission-lock options **[S-ST: F-11 item 7]**; a brand licence and transfer-pricing discipline **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**; a holding structure **[R4 (iii)]**; federation at scale **[§5 Scenario D]**; seven reserved matters **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; Krytos as a candidate funder **[S-B: E-03, B-03]**.

## §4 — What the corpus assumes

**Eighteen assumptions (Part II, Model C).** Seventeen are load-bearing. The five the brief forbids assuming are classified as follows:

| Forbidden assumption | Classification | Where the corpus relies on it |
|---|---|---|
| **Krytos = a company** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** (C-2) | *"a corporate owner"* **[S-E: Rule E-58 rationale]**; *"golden-share/protective-vote"*, *"no single shareholder controls"* **[S-ST: F-11]** |
| **Krytos = a parent company** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** (C-1, C-7) | *"subsidiary"*, *"Internal Krytos division"*, *"holding structure"* |
| **Krytos is already legally constituted** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** (C-11, C-13) | *"Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]"*; *"a future owner of Krytos"*; *"sells Krytos"* |
| **The founder owns Krytos** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** (C-3) | *"one person's ownership of the parent"*; *"under one private owner"*; *"Whoever owns Krytos"* |
| **Sewak Sathi is legally subordinate to Krytos** | **`SOURCE-ASSUMED`** (C-6) | the preposition *"under"* in eleven places; *"sits inside the broader Krytos ecosystem"*; *"a component owned by Krytos"* |

**None of the five is established anywhere. All five are relied upon.**

## §5 — What is unknown

**Fifteen categories (Part II, Model D)** and **twenty-eight questions (Part III)**, of which **one is answered** — the founder's given name. **No Krytos constitutive document exists in the corpus. Four upstream documents are named and none is supplied. Krytos has no governance content: zero of twelve attributes, no row in either authority instrument, no RACI line, no officers, no succession provision, no responsibility, no output, no escalation route.**

## §6 — The eight relationship models

**Part IV.** **R1 independent · R2 parent/controlled · R3 common control · R4 founder-linked but independent · R5 shared services (overlay) · R6 IP/licensing (overlay) · R7 multi-entity group · R8 other/undefined.**

**R8 is the present state.** **R2 is the assumed default. R3 is the model the ownership language most often implies. R4 is the model §3.1's exact words best support.** **None is chosen, none is excluded, and the corpus never compares them.**

## §7 — The consequences

**240 cells (Part V).** Distribution: **69 `SOURCE-SUPPORTED` · 35 `SOURCE-PROPOSED` · 57 `UNKNOWN` · 59 `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` · 20 `STRUCTURALLY DEPENDENT`.** **83 mandatory-marker instances across 72 distinct cells — 30% of the matrix.**

**R8 carries 18 `UNKNOWN` cells; R1 and R4 carry 4 each.** **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION` appears at HD-01, HD-03 and HD-27 in all eight models — the three dimensions on which everything else depends.**

## §8 — The four pillars

**Part VI.** **No pillar is declared satisfied or unsatisfied by any model.** **No model leaves all four pillars unconditioned.** **R2 is the only model with a source-recorded tension in three of the four — in the corpus's own risk column at [S: App. A §3.2 row 1] and at [S-ST: §18.2].** **R8 leaves all four `UNKNOWN`.** **The protection pillar is the least able to tolerate that: cover is written on a legal person, and none exists.**

## §9 — Money, IP and data

- **Money (Part X):** **eight flows, 64 cells. One flow is permitted by an explicit source. Every flow requires an agreement that does not exist and tax validation that has not been sought. The founder is a related party in all eight models.** **No amount, rate or threshold appears anywhere, and none may be invented.**
- **IP (Part VIII):** **seventeen asset classes. Zero have a stated owner. Five have custody or a function recorded instead — and custody is not ownership.** The corpus's two statements about the operating system **conflict** (**H-PD-16**).
- **Data (Part IX):** **eleven classes, 77 cells. Seventy are `UNKNOWN`.** No retention period, no deletion route and no breach responsibility exists for any class. **The only role statement in the corpus is *"presumably … ultimately Krytos."***

## §10 — Governance and succession

- **Governance (Part XI):** **Krytos adds a layer in position and in nothing else.** Above the CEO in three of five hierarchies; **zero of twelve governance attributes provided.** It does not resolve `LDR-U01` — **it relocates it above the Council, to a place with no rules.** **`CEO vs National Council — AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` is preserved verbatim.**
- **Succession (Part XII):** **eleven scenarios, 99 cells. The corpus supplies a determinate answer in nine.** **One mechanism exists — Art.19.** **No recovery route exists in any scenario, and in five the corpus records that none exists.** Three scenarios are never contemplated; two of them are worse than the one that is.

## §11 — What must be established

**Part XV: KDP-01 … KDP-12.** **Ten disclosures, two decisions.** **All ten disclosures are held by one person. None requires a professional, a budget, a vote or an architectural change.**

**KDP-01** legal status · **KDP-02** jurisdiction · **KDP-03** governing documents *(including the unsupplied v1.0 specification)* · **KDP-04** ownership and control · **KDP-05** succession · **KDP-06** IP · **KDP-07** brand · **KDP-08** data authority · **KDP-09** banking and funding authority · **KDP-10** the relationship as a fact *(including the four sibling projects)* · **KDP-11** Krytos's authority over Sewak Sathi *(decision)* · **KDP-12** Sewak Sathi's independence conditions *(decision)*.

**Part XVII: ten disclosers, twelve validators, no implementers, no auditors.** **For KDP-11 and KDP-12 the DECIDER is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`.**

## §12 — The decision required, and by whom

**[P]** The output chain is preserved exactly: **OPTION SET → CONSEQUENCE ANALYSIS → PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION → HUMAN DECISION.**

| Step | Status |
|---|---|
| **OPTION SET** | **Complete.** R1…R8 (Part IV), with the four source structure options at **[S: App. A §3.2]** mapped onto them |
| **CONSEQUENCE ANALYSIS** | **Complete.** 240 cells (Part V), 32 pillar cells (Part VI), 64 money cells (Part X), 77 data cells (Part IX), 99 succession cells (Part XII), 7 independence conditions (Part XIII), 16 control domains (Part XIV), 14 pilot domains (Part XX) |
| **PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION** | **Cannot begin.** **48 questions across 8 packs, none answerable today (H-PD-31).** Three opinions defined — **H-PV-1, H-PV-2, H-PV-3** — each conditional on KDP-01…KDP-04 |
| **HUMAN DECISION** | **Cannot be taken.** KDP-11 and KDP-12 have **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** deciders |

**The decision required now is not a structural decision. It is a disclosure.** **KDP-01…KDP-04 make the structural decision possible; they do not make it.**

**[P] This Part is not legal advice, not tax advice, not a recommendation, and not a selection.** **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** · **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** · **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`** · **`STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** · **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** apply throughout. **No permission to operate informally is granted or implied. A-N16 is preserved as a BLOCKER.**
